...not a weed / which with a good conscience cannot be tolerated / as it was condemned by the Council of Trent <term>Concilio Tridentino: The 16th-century ecumenical council that defined Catholic doctrine in opposition to the Protestant Reformation</term> and the Imperial Constitutions <term>Reichsconstitutionen: The fundamental laws and decrees of the Holy Roman Empire</term> / just as Heathenry itself / which Martin Luther himself confesses / that he received from the Devil / and which must be eradicated by all means through cunning and force: Even His Electoral Highness <note>Likely referring to John George I, the Elector of Saxony, who attempted to maintain a neutral or moderate path during the war</note> is here treated to a magnificent "stroking of the fox-tail" <note>An idiomatic expression meaning gross flattery or deception</note> and credited with / Sincerity / Bravery / and an upright German spirit: which, although it is the truth / one might yet say to those from whom such praise ever originates:

->Those are the worst cats,<-
->Who lick in front, and scratch behind.<-

For what the Catholic side truly thinks not only of this highly praiseworthy Elector, and how they speak behind the backs of him and other protesting German princes and estates, of that a whole book could be written: the clear, undeniable written word proves it; for the sake of preferred brevity, we will cite only a few examples for this time.

Daniel Eremita <note>A Flemish-born diplomat and writer who served the Medici and later wrote about the German courts</note> describes His Electoral Highness thus in the aforementioned epistle: "The reddish spots that frequently appear on his face—no obscure signs of cruelty and ferocity—cloud his features like a smoky mist; and though these signs might be deceptive, his life is in no way different from them." <note>original Latin: *Illi subinde in vultum incurrentes rubidæ maculæ, crudelitatis & immanitatis haud obscura indicia, oris lineamenta veluti fulgineâ nube confundunt á quibus etsi fallacibus argumentis, vita eius neutiquam discrepat.*</note> Carolus Caraffa, the Papal nuncio <term>Nuncio: A high-ranking diplomatic representative of the Pope</term> at the Imperial Court, in his letter to the Papal Nuncio in Brussels writes: "The Lutherans and those who remain neutral, however, have neither power nor courage." *A little later* <note>original Latin: *paulò post*</note>, he writes: "The Elector of Saxony is no prince of such high spirit or great power that the Catholic side should fear him much."

Caspar Scioppius <note>A notorious Catholic polemicist known for his aggressive rhetoric against Protestants</note> in the first and following chapters of the *Clarion Call* <note>original Latin: *Classicum*, a trumpet signal for battle</note> of this present Holy War <note>original Latin: *Belli sacri*</note>, calls the protesting Princes and Cities—who changed the Papal religion and enjoy the use of church properties—Heretics / Unjust / Disobedient Usurpers <term>Bergwältiger: A term for those who take possession of property (specifically church lands) through force or illegal seizure</term> /

...we can remind the reader of such a thing here, regarding the little word of the "commonwealth" <note>original: "gemeinen wesens" — referring to the shared public interest or the state.</note> sent ahead in the beginning of this latest **Letter of Sincerity** <note>original: "Sincerir Brieff" — a formal diplomatic letter intended to demonstrate one’s "sincere" intentions or to clear oneself of suspicion.</note>: for once the **Elector of Mainz** <note>original: "Chur Mentz" — the Archbishop of Mainz, who was one of the highest-ranking princes and electors of the Holy Roman Empire.</note> confesses his adherence to the **Council of Trent** <note>original: "Concilio Tridentino" (1545–1563) — the definitive ecclesiastical council of the Counter-Reformation which condemned Protestant doctrines.</note> and its observation or execution, how can he then include the **Protestant Estates** <note>original: "Protestirende Stände" — the princes and cities of the Empire who followed the Reformation.</note> in Germany within that "commonwealth"? He must therefore limp on either this side or that <note>An idiom meaning he is caught in a contradiction or trying to walk two paths at once.</note>. ### Ecclesiasticus Chapter 28, verse 15 <note>The author cites the Book of Sirach (also known as Ecclesiasticus), a book of the Apocrypha/Deuterocanon often used in the period to provide moral instruction on speech and social conduct.</note>

> **Curse the whisperer and the double-tongued** <note>original: "Susurronem ac bilinguem exsecramini"</note>: for they have destroyed many who were at peace. A **double tongue** <note>original: "Lingua duplex"</note> has troubled many, and scattered them from one nation to another: and it has pulled down fortified cities, and overthrown the houses of the great. A double tongue has cast out brave women and deprived them of their labors. He who gives heed to it shall never find rest, nor shall he dwell in tranquility. The stroke of a whip leaves a mark, but the **stroke of the tongue breaks bones**. Many have fallen by the edge of the sword, but not so many as have fallen because of the tongue. Blessed is he who is protected against it, who has not experienced its fury: who has not drawn its yo- <note>The word "yoke" (Latin: "jugum") is cut off and continues on the next page.</note>

<summary>The author accuses the Elector of Mainz of "double-speaking" by professing friendship to Protestant states while remaining bound by the anti-Protestant decrees of the Council of Trent, concluding with a biblical warning against the "double tongue."</summary>
<keywords>Elector of Mainz, Council of Trent, Letter of Sincerity, Protestant Estates, double-tongue, Ecclesiasticus, religious polemic, Counter-Reformation</keywords>

--- Page 37 ---
<meta>This page marks the conclusion of the treatise. It completes a Latin scriptural warning—likely adapted from the Book of Sirach (Ecclesiasticus) 21—regarding the spiritual bondage of the wicked, before transitioning into a German rhyming exhortation. The author concludes by urging the reader to shun the Catholic clergy, whom he equates with the "Antichrist," and to remain active in both prayer and labor.</meta>

<page-num>III.</page-num>

...[the yo]ke of it, and is not bound by its chains. For its yoke is an iron yoke <note>original: "iugum ferreum"</note> and its chains are bronze chains <note>original: "vincula ærea"</note>. The death brought by it is most evil, and the grave is more useful than it. It shall by no means rule over the godly, and they shall by no means be burned by its flame. Those who forsake the Lord shall fall into it; and in them it shall be kindled and never extinguished; it shall be sent upon them like a lion <note>original: "Leo"</note>, and like a leopard <note>original: "Pardalis"</note> it shall destroy them.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Here you have now in summary,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The nature of the Antichrist <term>Antichristenthumb — the kingdom or dominion of the Antichrist, a common Reformation label for the Papacy and its structures</term> shown skillfully;
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;If you have understood me correctly,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Then you will flee the race of priests <term>Pfaffen Gschlecht — a derogatory collective term for the Catholic clergy, implying they are a different "tribe" or "species" from the common people</term>.
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;If you help them, you withdraw help from yourself,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;I beg of you, take care,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;So that misfortune does not catch you;
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Pray diligently, and use your hands briskly <note>A call to be both spiritually and physically active in one’s defense or vocation.</note>.

->T H E  E N D.<-

<summary>This final page concludes a polemic against the Catholic hierarchy, using biblical imagery of iron yokes and wild beasts to warn of the spiritual dangers of "Antichristian" rule. It ends with a vernacular rhyme advising the reader to avoid the clergy and remain steadfast in prayer and work.</summary>

### To

# **Sincere / Pious /**

## **True German Patriots, lovers of truth and honorers of the Fatherland / My respectively Gracious / Highly-favorable Gentlemen.**

**Highly honored / Much-trusted Gentlemen / Well-proven Friends:** My dutiful compliance in maintaining your well-being, as well as [securing your] grace and high favor, remains pledged and obligated to serve your Christian constancy and laudable **religious zeal** for all the days of my life. <note>original Latin: *ad dies vitæ*, a legalistic phrase meaning for the duration of one's life.</note>

Many faithful **supporters** <note>original: *Favoriten*. In the 17th century, this often referred to political allies or patrons rather than "favorites" in the modern sense.</note> have very zealously petitioned me for the **publication** of the **Supplement to the Roman-Spanish Chancellery**, so much so that they imagined for themselves that I had taken their requests poorly or misunderstood them. This was certainly not the case; rather, I recognized in them a faithfully zealous, truly German, and patriotic spirit. However, the reason that this [publication] has been somewhat delayed is not only my **physical infirmity** <note>original: *Leibs Blödigkeit*. In this era, *Blödigkeit* referred to bodily weakness, frailty, or exhaustion, rather than the modern German meaning of stupidity.</note> but also primarily because one [must]...

<summary>In this preface, the author addresses his "Patriotic" Protestant readers, apologizing for the delay in publishing this supplement and attributing the pause to his poor health and the complexity of the material.</summary>
<keywords>patriotism, Protestantism, Roman-Spanish Chancellery, publication history, 1624, religious conflict</keywords>

...men more than they fear God, and 
therefore it has been difficult and dangerous 
to advance, proceed, and succeed with it. 
But Almighty God still always has His 
faithful servants, who do not bow their knees before Baal, 
<note>A biblical reference to those who refuse to worship false idols. In Protestant rhetoric of this era, "Baal" often represented the Pope or the Roman Catholic Church.</note>

<note>original: *Hasenforcht*. An idiomatic term for extreme cowardice or "chicken-heartedness."</note>

so has He also sent such to me for the advancement 
of this work. My own inability indeed 
prevents me from earning their favor, 
but on the other hand, I am in full and 
rich certainty that those whom it concerns—

whose honor, reputation, and majesty, 
welfare and innocence are at stake—will 
most graciously acknowledge and reciprocate this.

keep, and receive great thanks, immortal 
fame, and a stately testimony of their true 
Christianity from all <term>orthodox</term> and pious hearts.

--- Page 50 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's address to "True German Patriots." The catchword from the previous page, "To the," leads into the final paragraph of this formal dedication. The author is framing his upcoming publication as a piece of evidence-based political defense for the Protestant cause during the early stages of the Thirty Years' War.</meta>

For better information, I intend to publish an **appendix** <note>original: *appendicem*</note> and supplement to the **Roman-Spanish Chancellery** <note>The "Roman-Spanish Chancellery" was a famous polemical work that claimed to expose a secret Catholic conspiracy to dominate Europe.</note> at the next opportunity. In this, I will—among other things—**demonstrate most clearly** <note>original Latin: *manifestissimè dociren*</note> with impressive **documents** <note>original: *documentis*</note> that the hindrance of peace has not existed at all on the side of the **Electorate of the Palatinate** <note>original: *Chur Pfaltz*. The Palatinate was the territory of the Protestant "Winter King" Frederick V, whose defeat was a central conflict of this era.</note>, but rather has been on the side of the **Papists** <note>A derogatory term for Catholics, used here to suggest they are more loyal to the Pope than to the German Empire.</note> at all times, even until this very hour.

Accompanying this supplement, there will also be found the **Considerations** <note>original: *Considerationes*</note> of a **Romanist** <note>A Catholic, specifically one who supports the Roman system.</note> (**Loyal to the Emperor**) <note>original Latin: *Cæsaris Fidelis*</note>, regarding why the Emperor, the Duke of Bavaria, and all Papists should reconcile themselves to peace. I hope it will serve everyone for information, warning, and oversight. I leave all of this to be judged by the patriotic reader's inextinguishable and immortal **fidelity** <note>original: *Fidelitet*</note> and loyalty; and hereby I commend all true Christians to the grace of Christ, but the **Belialites** <note>original: *Belialiten*. A biblical term for those who are lawless or follow the devil (Belial), used here to demonize the author's political opponents.</note> to the disgrace of their Belial.

->Truth endures,<-
->Lies scatter like dust.<-

--- Page 51 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the author's introductory dedication to the formal "Entrance" or beginning of the main text. The previous page concluded with a promise to provide evidence that the Catholic side ("Papists") was responsible for the lack of peace in the Empire. This page opens with a scathing polemical poem addressed to the Jesuits, using the historical example of the fall of Antwerp to warn Lutherans against making deals with Catholic forces.</meta>

## To the Jesu-Opponents <note>original: *Jesuwieder*. This is a derogatory pun on "Jesuit," combining *Jesus* with *wider* (against), implying they are the enemies of Christ rather than his followers.</note>.

You have set your minds—to further your cause—
To make the **Empire** <note>The Holy Roman Empire.</note> the property of **Austria** <note>Referring to the House of Habsburg, the ruling dynasty of the Empire and the primary champions of the Catholic Counter-Reformation.</note>,
  And to plant nothing but **idolatry** <note>original: *Abgötterey*. A standard Protestant term for Catholic rituals, particularly the Mass and the veneration of saints.</note> forevermore,
  From West to East, from South to North.
For this reason, you have now confused the whole world,
So that one strays here, and another there.
  You cast the blame for it all onto the **Calvinists** <note>One of the two major branches of Protestantism; at this time, they lacked the same legal protections as Lutherans within the Empire.</note> so neatly;
  They must now serve as the cover for your **wickedness**.
The things you have instigated, you blame on them;
You say there must be unrest because they are tolerated in the Empire.
  Just like **Emperor Nero**, who set Rome on fire,
  And blamed the Christians, saying they had done it.
You name only one group, yet you mean both <note>Meaning both the Lutherans and the Calvinists.</note>;
The unity between the two brings sorrow to your hearts.
  Therefore you seek with cunning how you might separate them,
  And bring one after the other into your power by force.
Forty years ago, you arranged this very same scheme
In the city of **Antwerp** <note>original: *Antorff*. This refers to the 1585 Siege of Antwerp during the Dutch Revolt, where Catholic Spanish forces successfully reclaimed the city and forced Protestants to convert or leave.</note>—the same fate now awaited in the Empire.
  You made the **Lutherans**, under a fine pretense,
  Agree with you on a common cause:
To show the Calvinists the door together.
As the Lutherans then believed that the sky was **full of violins** <note>original: *es hieng voll Geygen*. A German idiom meaning that everything seemed perfect or that they were in a state of blissful success.</note>
  On their behalf—you then showed them
  What you had already done to the others with their help.
The **Evil One** <note>Likely referring to the Jesuits or the Devil guiding them.</note> proceeds boldly; you might well succeed.
Yet in the end, it will all fall back upon your own backs.
  When you finally believe you are out of danger,
  So God, the righteous God, will truly punish you most dreadfully.
  For thus the Lord uses His power forevermore.

<summary>This page contains a polemical poem titled "To the Jesu-Opponents," which accuses the Jesuit order and the Habsburg dynasty of conspiring to conquer the Holy Roman Empire. The author warns Lutherans that the Catholics are using "divide and conquer" tactics, citing the 1585 fall of Antwerp as a historical warning of what happens when Protestant factions fail to remain united.</summary>

<keywords>Jesuits, House of Habsburg, Counter-Reformation, Calvinism, Lutheranism, Thirty Years' War, Antwerp, religious polemic, Emperor Nero</keywords>

Just like **Emperor Nero**, who set Rome on fire,
And blamed the Christians, saying they had done it.
You name only one group, yet you mean both <note>The author argues that while Jesuits attack Calvinists specifically, their ultimate goal is to destroy all Protestants, including Lutherans.</note>;
The unity between the two brings sorrow to your hearts.
Therefore you seek with cunning how you might separate them,
And bring one after the other into your power by force.
Forty years ago you plotted this very same thing
In the city of **Antwerp** <note>original: *Antorff*. Referring to the Fall of Antwerp in 1585, a major turning point in the Dutch Revolt where Catholic Spanish forces reclaimed the city.</note>; what is now expected in the Empire.
You made the **Lutherans**, under a fine appearance,
Agree to make common cause with you,
To show the Calvinists the door altogether.
When the Lutherans then believed that **the sky was full of fiddles** <note>original: *es hieng voll Geygen / Der Himmel*. A German idiom meaning to be overly optimistic or to believe everything is perfect and wonderful.</note>
On their behalf: then you showed them
What you had done to those others before with their help.
The wickedness proceeds bravely; you might well succeed,
But in the end, it will turn out [badly] upon your own backs.
When you fully believe that you are out of danger,
Then God, the righteous God, will punish you horribly indeed.
For the Lord uses His power forevermore.

<summary>A polemical poem warning Lutherans that the Jesuits are using "divide and conquer" tactics—citing the 1585 Fall of Antwerp as a cautionary tale—to bring the Holy Roman Empire under total Habsburg and Catholic control.</summary>
<keywords>Jesuits, House of Habsburg, Calvinists, Lutherans, Antwerp, religious polemic, Thirty Years' War context</keywords>

# Introduction

## To the impartial, peace-loving reader who hungers for the truth.

Just as it is a certain sign of a corpse in a house when one sees the gravediggers, pallbearers, <term>funeral attendants <note>original: leichfänger; refers to minor officials who assisted with funeral processions</note></term>, and similar birds of death wandering inside: so too is it in the <term>commonweal <note>original: gemeinem Wesen; the public body or state</note></term> an infallible omen of a certain downfall—or at least a significant alteration and disruption of the government and the entire state—when one sees foreigners rising up and gaining the upper hand in the land. 

Understand this to mean not only an overcrowding of foreign subjects and the granting of citizenship to outsiders, but much more and primarily, the use of foreign advisors. It is a sign of decay when foreigners are drawn into the most prominent offices and <term>councils <note>original: Consiliis</note></term>, when foreign aid and troops are led into the country, or when such people are lured in or sheltered who are promoters and negotiators for the well-known foreign "lust for power," or who otherwise receive secret gifts, pensions, and annual payments from such foreign <term>potentates <note>monarchs or powerful rulers</note></term>.

And it is exactly this which has been one of the primary <term>grievances <note>original: Gravaminum; legal or political complaints presented to a ruler</note></term> in the <term>Empire <note>The Holy Roman Empire</note></term>, which the <term>Estates <note>The representative ranks of the Empire, such as princes, nobles, and clergy</note></term> have complained about from of old until this very hour—though in vain. For instead of these foreigners being turned away from the Imperial Court, it seems that they have not only gained total control and governance there, but have also, through strange and unnoticed schemes, done the same in almost the majority of <term>Evangelical <note>Protestant</note></term> princely courts.

<summary>This preface warns that the rise of foreign advisors and mercenaries within the Holy Roman Empire is a primary cause of political decay and a sign of imminent collapse. It specifically identifies foreign influence in both the Imperial Court and Protestant princely courts as a long-standing but unaddressed grievance.</summary>
<keywords>foreign influence, political decay, Imperial Court, Protestant princes, grievances, Holy Roman Empire, advisors, statecraft</keywords>

insinuated themselves. And this—namely the interference of foreign nations in the governance of the <term>Empire <note>The Holy Roman Empire</note></term>—already began in the time of **Emperor Charles** <note>Charles V (r. 1519–1556), who ruled a vast global empire including Spain, the Netherlands, and parts of Italy</note>, from which two great evils for the **German Nation** resulted! *See Lazarus von Schwendi in his discourse on the welfare of the Holy Roman Empire:* Namely, first, because the Germans are by nature and ancient descent devoted to liberty and have never allowed any foreign nation to rule them, they conceived a secret resentment against the government of their Lord, **Emperor Charles**.

Secondly, that such foreign nations in their counsels and undertakings did not look toward the Imperial office and majesty, nor toward the honor and best interests of the **German Nation**, but rather had their own particular goals and intentions. They sought ways to make the **Pope**, **Burgundy**, and **Spain** great, and to bring the affairs of the Empire into their own hands for their own use and advantage. Furthermore, such people, during occurring Imperial proceedings and deliberations on how to counter the encroaching defects and to honor **Emperor Charles**, did not advise on sensible and timely means and ways. Nor did they permit the proposals and means suggested by other well-meaning **Estates of the Empire** to be accepted. Instead, they had their own private concerns and secretly wished to see **Germany** fall into internal **division and schism**, so that it might be weakened and stirred up to internal war. Meanwhile, they could more more easily set foot in it, attach one side or party to themselves, and subsequently conquer the other.

This is admitted by ***Windek in his Deliberation on the extirpation of heresies <note>original: Deliberation de hæresibus extirpandis</note>, Antidote II, no. 8,*** where he says: that **Emperor Charles** subdued the **Protestants** by this very means—by using the secret resentment and disagreements they had among themselves to his advantage and separating them from one another, so that they could neither with deed nor

Since this devilish trick has succeeded so grandly, it is now being used once again in the same way. Daily experience proves that all of this is still practiced in the **Empire** today and is in full swing; for one unfortunately learns that now the **Spanish**, **Papal**, and other foreign ambassadors rule almost the entire **Empire**. As they pipe in **Spain** and **Rome**, so must we dance in the **German Empire**. (See the letter from the Papal **Nuncio** <note>A permanent diplomatic representative of the Holy See</note> in **Vienna** to the Capuchin **Hyacinth** <note>Hyacinth of Casale (1575–1627), an influential diplomat and monk who worked to further the interests of the Catholic League</note>, number 12, in the *Preface to the Roman-Spanish Chancellery*.)

Through this foreign interference, things have unfortunately reached the point that—just as in ancient times among the **Greeks**, after they had adopted too many foreign customs, vices, and habits—**Greek faith** <note>original: *Græca fides*; an ancient Roman proverb implying that Greeks were untrustworthy</note>, which was initially celebrated as a proverb and held to be the most firm, was afterwards overturned and likewise proverbially decried as the most trivial and worthless.

Constitutions; especially **concerning Religious and Public Peace** <note>original: "de pace Religiosâ & Publicâ"</note>, the treaties of Ulm, Bingen, Mainz, Aschaffenburg, and other agreements established until now. Contrast these with daily proceedings, experience, the **Spanish Chancery** <note>The administrative office of the Spanish crown, here representing foreign interference in German laws</note> and its vanguard, and the writings contained therein. Look purely for the sake of God and our own temporal and eternal welfare, to see whether there is even one article, one point, or one chapter that has not been destroyed, riddled with holes, broken, and thrown into a heap by the aforementioned foreign **counselors** <note>original: "Consiliarios"</note>—indeed, whether they are not almost mocked and laughed at?

What is more: they are now beginning to derive **Imperial Justice** <note>original: "Iustitiam cæsaream"</note> no longer from the **Golden Bull** <note>The fundamental law of the Holy Roman Empire, issued in 1356, which settled the method of electing the Emperor</note>, the **Imperial Constitutions** <note>original: "Reichs Constitutionen"</note>, or the **Capitulations** <note>The binding contracts signed by an Emperor before his election</note>—which are the fundamental laws of the Empire—but rather **from Papal Bulls, and from dialectical and rhetorical rules, etc.** <note>original: "ex Bullis Papalibus, ex præceptis Dialecticis & Rhetoricis"</note>.

Thus it seems almost foolish that those on the **Protestant side** <note>original: "Evangelischen theils"</note> would dwell so much on this and try to persuade themselves that these people—having begun to recast the entire Empire into a new mold—will pay any regard to those Imperial Constitutions, Golden Bulls, and other Imperial statutes, which in their eyes are obsolete and void. Even more foolish seem those cities and **Estates** <note>original: "Ständ"; the various territories and cities that had a seat in the Imperial Diet</note> who, following the changes introduced to the Empire today, would cite their **private liberties and privileges** <note>original: "privat Freyheiten vnd Privilegien"</note> before these newly-minted foreign builders of monarchies and devious **crown-eaters** <note>original: "Cronen Fressern"; a vivid metaphor for foreign powers devouring the sovereignty of German princes</note>. 

Before foreign generals, colonels, war officers, and the foreign nations now prevailing in the Empire, they might try to use their rights and old, neglected privileges as a defense—as if there were **rights among soldiers, or laws amidst arms** <note>original: "inter milites jura, inter arma leges"; a variation of the proverb "In times of war, the laws fall silent"</note>. To these people, who are foreign and ignorant of the German character—or perhaps even defiant, or pretending ignorance if they do know—such excuses seem only mocking and ridiculous; indeed, childish. For it is, in truth, ridiculous in itself to say much of this or that particular freedom when the **universal freedom** <note>original: "universalfreyheit"; the collective liberty of the Empire as a whole</note>...

<meta>This page continues the author's polemic against the Catholic counter-reformation efforts within the Holy Roman Empire. Inquisition, completed in the Empire (in which, anyway, the complete Papacy has already been reintroduced in the most prominent provinces) established and executed according to the contents of the Council of Trent <note>original Latin: *secundum tenorem Concilii Tridentini*. The Council of Trent (1545–1563) was the Catholic Church's formal response to the Reformation, clarifying doctrines and initiating the Counter-Reformation.</note>.

Now, there are still many on the Protestant <note>original: *Evangelischen*; in this period, it refers generally to the Lutheran side of the Reformation</note> side, it is to be regretted, who will not learn wisdom even from harm, but rather, if one only gives them money and good words, and—as it is called—"sincerizes" <note>The author uses the term *sinceriret*, a Germanized version of a Latin root, to describe a political tactic of feigning "sincerity" or honest intentions as a deceptive mask</note> with them, they might have been deceived ninety-nine times by such double-tongued behavior, yet they would let themselves be deceived for the hundredth time (if only they did not fear being laughed at by the Calvinists for it). Yet a rational person should look much more at the deeds of his opponent than at his simulated words and appearance, for it is said: **by their fruits you shall know them.** My dear pious German countryman, by their fruits you must recognize what they are plotting <note>literally "carrying in their shield," a heraldic reference to one's hidden intentions or coat of arms</note> and what sticks hidden behind their glistening words: a simple man tells you this faithfully: Now these things are done purely and holily <note>original Latin: *Iam Purè, Sanctéq; Hæc*; likely used sarcastically to mimic the tone of the Catholic authorities</note>.

Therefore, do not let yourself be misled by this seemingly sincere, yet snake-stinging and poisonous "sincerizing." But if you do so, and show yourself to be either indifferent and Neutral in the matters of your highest Lord and Master in Heaven (which hardly appears Christian), or otherwise scheme and flatter his and your common enemy, then he will let you die at his hands. Peace is to be cultivated where we do not suffer the loss of God; conversely, war is to be waged where <term>Religion</term> <note>The author uses the Latin term *Religio* to emphasize that the core of the conflict is the survival of the faith itself</note> is in danger <note>original Latin: *Pax colenda est ubi Dei jacturam non facimus, rursus bellum gerendum, ubi Religio periclitatur*</note>. For the fact that the opponent desires to have you on his side does not happen for the sake of your golden hair <note>an idiom meaning "for your own sake" or "out of kindness"</note> but for your destruction, and not at all for your benefit. If you do it, however, you act like a foolish goose who stuck her neck into the throat of the hungry fox (who simulated that he had a bone stuck in his throat); but he, despite having strongly "sincerized," bit off her neck and ate her. That was the reward he gave the goose for her help; of such a reward you can certainly expect, and no other.

<meta>This page continues the author's warning to fellow Protestants against believing the peaceful pretenses of Catholic powers. The previous page ended with an exhortation to recognize the true intentions of the opposition by their "fruits" rather than their "simulated words."</meta>

just as you can now sufficiently calculate this from practice and the outcome—experience being the teacher of fools <note>original Latin: *ex praxi & eventu, stultorum magistro*</note>—and from the clear confession of the zealous Catholic Windeck <note>Paul Windeck was a Catholic polemicist known for his sharp attacks on Protestantism</note> cited above. Even if they speak fair words when they are among us, it is well known what kind of talk circulates when they are alone among themselves. Furthermore, some from their own midst cannot remain silent—indeed, the works and violent acts they have committed against us until now generally reveal—how they truly feel in their hearts. **A lie is thin; it can be seen through.** <note>original Latin: *Tenue est mendacium, pellucet*, a quote from the Roman philosopher Seneca.</note>

They have already told, written, and sung it to us often enough before this. People have long seen and heard what they intended, and has it not now almost all happened according to their pleasure? For it happens daily, what Windeck prophesied to us in his *Prognosticon* regarding the future state of the Catholic Papal Church and the extermination of heretics; what the aforementioned Archduke Maximilian’s <note>Likely Maximilian III, Archduke of Austria (1558–1618), a staunch supporter of the Counter-Reformation</note> presentation to the Holy Roman Empire and his considerations on the succession of the House of Austria entail; what the alarm-blower Scioppius <note>Caspar Scioppius (1576–1649), a controversialist scholar and convert to Catholicism who advocated for a holy war against Protestants</note> proclaimed years ago in his battle-cry for a religious war; and what subtle advice Campanella <note>Tommaso Campanella (1568–1639), an Italian philosopher whose work *De Monarchia Hispanica* argued for a universal Spanish-Catholic empire</note> revealed in his *Spanish Monarchy*. These and many others have already painted the picture for us plainly and clearly <note>original: *teutsch*, literally "in German," used idiomatically here to mean "plainly" or "unambiguously"</note> in advance. 

And now we grasp the effect with our own hands, and feel the slaps to the face, and see the raised, bloodthirsty hands of our enemies, and yet we believe it is not meant maliciously as soon as they twist just one more fair word toward us. Indeed, there are many who turn entirely to their side under the pretext of maintaining the majesty of the authorities <note>original: *unter dem prætext, die Hochheit der Obrigkeit zumanuteniren*</note>, even though the fundamental cause of the whole endeavor aims at the elevation of the Papacy and the eradication of the Gospel. Some do it out of blindness and stubbornness; some for the sake of a handful of honor or "coined earth" <note>a derogatory term for money</note>; and some out of a "rabbit's fear" <note>cowardice</note> for the sake of a "hatful of flesh" <note>one's own physical life or body</note>. But it will happen to them as God the Lord says: because they [ignored] His express...

<meta>This page continues the author’s polemic against those who ignore the dangers of the Papacy. The previous page ended with a warning that the true intentions of the Catholic powers are revealed by their actions rather than their deceptive words.</meta>

II
B 2

...disobedient to the command, they do not go out from Babel <note>In Protestant rhetoric of this period, "Babel" or Babylon refers to the Roman Catholic Church</note>: but rather they flatter the Whore; indeed, they even help her, and they will perish along with her and her double-tongued duplicity. For God will certainly not leave all double tongues and false hearts unpunished, but will visit them suddenly, together with all those to whom He has now revealed the Antichrist through His grace and the light of the *Gospel* <note>original Latin: *Evangelij*</note>, but who still refuse to recognize him, choosing instead to be utterly stone-blind with seeing eyes.

Since God has commanded us to pray even for our enemies, how much more properly should we then pray for our high authorities? We pray that He might touch and guide, enlighten and open the mild Austrian heart of the pious Emperor <note>Likely referring to the Holy Roman Emperor, the head of the House of Austria</note>, so that he may maturely weigh and consider how faithfully the late, praiseworthy and dear Elector *Christian* of Saxony <note>Christian I (1560–1591) or Christian II (1583–1611) of Saxony, both of whom maintained complex alliances with the Habsburgs</note> warned the House of Austria that it has no worse or more harmful enemy than the very Throne of *Rome* and the *trading fathers* <note>original Latin: *patres negotiatores*</note>—that is, the Jesuit order which destroys leaf and grass—as the true tools and *henchmen* <note>original Latin: *Satellites*</note> of foreign tyranny and the lust for power. 

And how reasonably and wisely *Lazarus von Schwendi* <note>Lazarus von Schwendi (1522–1583) was a prominent diplomat and general who advised three Emperors to practice religious tolerance to preserve the Empire</note>, the faithful advisor to the aforementioned House, foresaw: that just as this House grew through the Empire and because it had the minds and approval of the Germans on its side, so too will it decline if it should lose the hearts of the Germans. Then, time and the final outcome will teach it how foolishly and poorly it acted by building upon and looking toward the connections, allegiance, and help of foreign nations—who seek only their own interests and desire to make themselves great, not others—rather than its own fatherland and the nation born to it and entrusted to its care. Truly, time and the work itself have already, to some extent, pointed this out as if with fingers...

But it will go for them as God the Lord says: that because they [disobeyed] His express...

<meta>This page continues the argument from the previous page, warning the Emperor that following the advice of foreign religious orders (the Jesuits) will lead to the ruin of his own lands and subjects.</meta>

...as was shown previously in **Switzerland** and the **Netherlands**: but now in **Hungary**, **Bohemia**, **Graubünden**, and the **Electoral Palatinate**; what damage and loss it can bring to a high authority and the Empire when they allow themselves to be embittered against their own nation and subjects—as if against their own children and members of their household—and incited to extremes by foreign, peace-hating, and rebellious people, of whatever rank they may be. For it was said not only to private individuals, but much more to the **fathers of the fatherland** <note>original Latin: *patribus patriae*; a classical title for rulers, emphasizing their duty to protect and nurture their people like a father</note>:

> You parents, provoke not your children to anger and impatience. <note>A reference to the biblical command in Ephesians 6:4</note>

To rely and base oneself on "good words" is otherwise to rely on very shaky pillars; we have certainly learned to our detriment how quickly one good word can be overturned by another word, or how a single imprisonment can be twisted into an eternal one. And who will believe words when the deeds testify to the contrary? In **Regensburg** <note>Referring to the Imperial Diet (Reichstag) held in Regensburg, where religious and political concessions were often debated between the Emperor and the Protestant estates</note>, everything that the **Evangelical** <note>The term used by Protestants—primarily Lutherans—to describe themselves</note> side could possibly wish for or desire was granted; but that they will also keep their word to us in this case—just as they have kept other and previous promises until now—is sufficiently revealed by some **Papists** <note>original German: *Bäpstischen*; a contemporary term for Roman Catholics</note> themselves. In the meantime, they lift the lid from the pot here and there (this treaty shall follow) and perhaps, out of a secret urge of conscience or else from imprudent and too-heated zeal, they "blab out of school" <note>original German idiom: *auß der schul schwetze*; to reveal secrets or speak more frankly than intended</note> and say somewhat more openly exactly what they have "in their craw" <note>original: *gefressen*; an idiom suggesting a hidden or swallowed grudge</note> for the **Evangelical** side.

The **Burgundian** <note>Referring to the envoys of the Spanish Habsburgs, who held the title of Dukes of Burgundy</note> envoys have declared and protested that their Lord desires to do no injury to religion, just as he does not do so in the **Palatinate**; but in the meantime, others are doing it in the said **Palatinate** and in **Bohemia**, and he himself does it in **Graubünden** <note>A region in present-day Switzerland; this refers to the "Grisons Bloodshed" (1620), where Protestants were massacred</note>. There, the poor people who do not wish to attend the **Mass** are driven out, scorched, beheaded, burned, and—in whole houses, churches, and schools full of people—are stabbed and butchered like irrational cattle.

<meta>This page continues the author's argument that the Catholic party, influenced by Jesuit theology, is systematically undermining the Peace of Augsburg. ...to drive out, persecute, and slaughter fellow believers, and yet claims to have done nothing against the **Peace of Religion** <note>original German: *Religionsfrieden*; referring to the 1555 Peace of Augsburg, which provided the legal framework for the coexistence of Catholicism and Lutheranism in the Holy Roman Empire</note>.

I cannot fathom it: unless it finally comes down to what the **Jesuits** generally teach: **that there is no peace with heretics—that is, with the Evangelicals—nor should faith be kept with them; furthermore, that under the term "Religion," one should not understand the heretical or so-called Evangelical faith, but only the one true Roman-Catholic Religion.** <note>original Latin: *Cum hæreticis, h.e. cum Evangelicis pacem nullam esse, nec fidem illis servandam, sed & sub vocabulo Religionis non intelligi hæreticam, sive prætensam Evangelicā, verùm unam veram Romano-Catholicam Religionem*</note>

Or, as **Windeck** <note>Johann Paul Windeck (d. 1614), a Catholic polemicist and counselor to the Archbishop of Cologne, known for his hardline stance against Protestantism</note> puts it in his *Prognostico*—he who certainly does not mince his words—when he writes: **"It is easily clear to a not-altogether-stupid reader from the decrees of the Imperial Diets <note>The *Reichstag*, the deliberative body of the Holy Roman Empire</note> that they grant free religion to no one, but have granted to the Protestants only a MORATORIUM, DELAY, AND TOLERANCE until a full decision of the case is clear to both parties. These parties wished the judgment to rest with a council, to which the adversaries promised obedience with pledged faith; but since they now refuse to keep it through stubborn rebellion, whatever troubles arise from the freedom of belief—which they hold by usurpation and not by right—must be attributed entirely to them."** <note>original Latin: *Ex Comitiorum Decretis non admodum stupido Lectori facile liquere, ea liberā religionem nemini indulgere...*</note> 

And he therefore wonders most greatly **at the madness of the sectarians** <note>original Latin: *de vecordiâ sectariorū*; "sectarian" was a derogatory Catholic term for Protestants</note> (as he speaks of us), **that we so often and most foolishly thunder and blather against the Catholics using the decrees of the Imperial Diets to defend free religion; whereas if we saw their meaning better and weighed it more precisely, we would notice that for them, the freedom of belief is being fundamentally uprooted rather than confirmed.** <note>original Latin: *quod pro liberâ religione tuendâ toties Comitiorum decreta contra Catholicos stultissimè detonare & deblaterare soleamus...*</note>

And this we unfortunately experience now in practice. For although those on the side of the higher acting persons do not yet want to speak out plainly **on this stage of our times** <note>original Latin: *in hac horum temporum scenâ*</note>, but rather still maneuver with words until they have completely **brought the birds to the trap** <note>original German: *vff den Kloben bring*; a metaphor for luring prey into a finished snare</note>, yet the work itself and, alas, the bloody outcome everywhere speaks for itself. They wanted...

<meta>This page continues the author's critique of the Catholic party’s political maneuvering. The previous page ended with a quote from the polemicist Windeck, suggesting that religious tolerance for Protestants was only ever intended as a temporary moratorium, not a permanent right.</meta>

Thus one sees that, for the time being, the Spanish **Inquisition** has not been abolished anywhere, but is instead being expanded ever further. In the same way, England is being lured with the promise of the **Restoration of the Electoral Palatinate**, though there is no intention of doing so. Meanwhile, those lands are being sucked dry to the very marrow. The pious, exiled—but not entirely driven away—**Count Palatine** <note>Frederick V (1596–1632), the Elector Palatine whose acceptance of the Bohemian crown sparked the Thirty Years' War</note>, along with his wife and children, are being pursued with all manner of murderous plots against their very lives. In practice, the electoral dignity and the land are being completely—lock, stock, and barrel—passed over by all the next-of-kin and relatives, and **actually transferred** <note>original Latin: *realiter transferirn*</note> to an **outsider** <note>original Latin: *Extraneum*; referring to Maximilian I of Bavaria, who was granted Frederick's electoral title and lands by the Emperor in 1623, an act of questionable legality at the time</note> and others through unusual processes and hasty manners. And yet, they still dare to say that such a move shall be **without prejudice** to the **Election Capitulation** <note>The *Wahlkapitulation*, a contract between the Holy Roman Emperor and the Electors</note>, the **Golden Bull** <note>The 1356 decree that served as the constitutional core of the Holy Roman Empire</note>, the **Imperial Constitutions**, or the **preeminence** and union of the **College of Electors**, etc.

Let someone make sense of that for me! For my part, I can neither make sense of nor reconcile such **subtleties**, **assurances**, **counter-assurances**, **legal contradictions**, or **contradictions** <note>original Latin: *Subtiliteten, Sincerationen, Antisincerationen, Antinomias oder contradictiones*</note>. Yet, I would certainly believe that today or tomorrow the Count Palatine's property might be returned to him—specifically, when **Spain** **restores** <note>original Latin: *restituiren*</note> and hands back the Kingdom of **Navarre** to its brother-in-law in France, and also returns the withheld lands to the innocent **Duke Louis Philip, Count Palatine** <note>Louis Philip (1602–1655), the younger brother of Frederick V, who also lost his lands during the conflict</note>.

One can place an equal (and worrisome) trust in the **assurances and promises** **not to disturb or violate religion and the religious peace** <note>original Latin: *Sincerationes vnd promissiones; de non turbandâ vel violandâ religione & pace religiosa*</note>. I would dearly like to **reconcile** <note>original: *concilijren*</note> the **proceedings** <note>original: *procedere*</note> carried out everywhere until now with these promises, if it were possible. But how strictly these recent promises of assurance will be kept in the future can easily be gathered if one looks back at how the past ones were kept. For let someone make sense of this for me: they continue to **reform** <note>original: *zureformiren*; used here ironically to describe the "Counter-Reformation" or the forced conversion of Protestant territories back to Catholicism</note> one land after another, here and there the religious—

<meta>This page continues the author's argument against the legal justifications used by the Catholic party to strip Protestants of their rights. The previous page ended by questioning the "subtlety" of legal maneuvers that bypassed imperial laws.</meta>

...readily claim that the Bohemians—through their resistance and rebellion (into which they were actually driven by overwhelming tyranny, coercion, and persecution, having been led so far out onto the edge and onto the **precipice of despair** <note>original Latin: *præcipitium desperationis*</note>, a choice of "bird, eat or die" <note>A German idiom meaning "submit or perish"</note>) that finally, **out of too much patience** <note>original Latin: *ex nimiâ patientiâ*</note>, a **madness** <note>original Latin: *furor*</note> must surely arise)—forfeited their **privileges**, the Letter of Majesty <note>The *Majestätsbrief* of 1609, a document granting religious tolerance to Bohemian Protestants</note>, and their religion.

But regarding this, let us ask the Catholic lords just a little **aside** <note>original Latin: *à part*</note> how this is reconciled: religious freedom is taken from the Bohemians because they resisted? Yet it was taken from the people of Styria, Graz, and others, and they did not resist. The churches in the city of Heidelberg are being closed **under this pretext** <note>original Latin: *hoc prætextu*</note> because the city was conquered by force. The churches of Prague and Donauwörth were closed even though they surrendered voluntarily. The people of the Grisons <note>The *Graubündtner*, inhabitants of the Swiss canton of Graubünden</note> have their ecclesiastical and **secular** <note>original Latin: *profan*</note> freedoms taken from them, and they neither resisted nor ever could—or even intended to—rise up against Spain or Austria (with whom they had no dealings, good or bad, and who have no business with them, being a free people).

Furthermore, the **patient and suffering** <note>original German/Latin hybrid: *patientirenden*</note> party would like to know in which **Imperial Constitution** <note>original Latin: *Reichsconstitution*</note> it is provided or written that when someone commits something punishable **politically** <note>original Latin: *politicè*</note> in the Empire, they should be punished for it **theologically** <note>original Latin: *Theologicè*</note> through the robbery of their worship and their freedom of conscience and religion? Come out with it, you Catholic-Romanists!

But perhaps it is not yet convenient for you that one speaks so loudly on your behalf; we must therefore **remain patient** <note>original German: *patientiren*</note> a while longer until the ulcer is fully ripe and, with God's help, bursts of its own accord. **In the meantime** <note>original Latin: *Interim*</note>, if this **type of punishment** <note>original Latin: *genus pœnæ*</note> is to become the norm, one might say (as that man once said: **"If to accuse is enough, who will be innocent?"** <note>original Latin: *si accusare sufficit, quis innocens erit?*</note>): **"If this is enough, O Protestant men, who among us will be innocent?"** <note>original Latin: *si hoc sufficit, ô viri Evangelici, quis nostrûm erit innocens?*</note> If that holds true, who will not be accused of "muddying the water" <note>An allusion to the fable of the Wolf and the Lamb; the author suggests the Catholics are looking for any excuse to attack</note>? Against whom will...

<meta>This page continues the author's critique of the legal and religious justifications used by Catholic authorities to strip Protestants of their rights. Having previously listed examples like Heidelberg and Prague where freedoms were revoked despite different circumstances, the author now argues that these "rebellions" are merely excuses for a long-planned eradication of the Protestant faith.</meta>

...one will not soon find and invent a **pretext** <note>original: *prætext*</note>, guilt, and accusation here and there, for the sake of which he will have to forfeit **freedom of conscience**? And because of this, they will rob entire lands of their well-established worship, and thus gradually seek to exterminate the supposed heretics under this **cloak** <note>original: *Deckmantel*, a metaphor for a disguise or false front</note> and seize the opportunity? **And so, as the legal scholars say: "that which cannot be done directly is done indirectly."** <note>original Latin: *Et sic, quod I. C. ajunt, fiet per obliquum, quod non potest fieri per directum.* I.C. refers to *Juris Consulti*, or experts in law.</note>

Furthermore, I ask only one more thing, which His **Electoral Highness** <note>original: *Churf: Durcht:*</note> of **Saxony**, as a highly intelligent German prince, has quite Christianly and rightly noted: Why then are the Catholics’ churches not also closed and their religion taken away when they commit offenses against the Empire or against **Austria**? Since, indeed, the greater part of the **Bohemians**—who were just as involved in these matters—are Catholic? Or can the Roman Catholics perhaps not commit offenses, just as their Church cannot err? Or must it perhaps be for them, as is the custom among the Roman Catholics, that it is said in this case: **"when two do the same thing, it is not the same."** <note>original Latin: *duo cum faciunt idem, non est idem*</note> From this, one must clearly observe that it is only intended and threatened as the **enforcement** <note>original: *Execution*</note> of the **Council of Trent** <note>The 16th-century Catholic council that defined the Counter-Reformation</note>, **under whatever pretext** <note>original: *sub quocunque prætextu*</note>, against the **Protestant religion** <note>original: *Evangelische Religion*</note>, as if against a **"fat goose"** <note>A German idiom for a tempting target or a victim ready to be plucked</note>. And therefore it remains true, as that man rightly says: **"The Catholics' intention is only to tolerate Protestants so long as they lack the opportunity and the power to remove them."** <note>original Latin: *Eousq; nempe Catholicis voluntas est tolerandi protestantes, donec eos tollendi fit occasio & facultas.*</note> **In short** <note>original: *In summa*</note>, it is just as they want it to be? **"Let the will stand in place of reason."** <note>original Latin: *sit pro ratione voluntas*, a quote from Juvenal implying that power, not logic, dictates the law.</note> This must finally be the **trump card** <note>original: *Stichblat*</note>, if everything is allowed to proceed this way, according to the appearance of these foreign advisors and their impetuous, irresponsible **rashness** <note>original: *præcipitantz*</note>:

->"**Thus we will, and thus we command.**"<-
<note>original Latin: *Sic volumusq; jubemusq;*</note>

But they would have good reason not to hurry so much, but...

He highlights the hypocrisy of punishing Protestant Bohemians while Catholic participants in the same events face no religious penalties.</summary>

...but rather to overcome their own heated **counsels** <note>original Latin: *Consilia*</note> and temperaments, and the stubborn foresight of their evil desires, to keep them in check, and perhaps to think back on the **outcomes of the past** <note>original Latin: *exitus præteritorum*</note> and on these words of their fellow believers, where he says: 

> Whoever attempts to change the entire religion of a people all at once, even a people who have been conquered, I shall wonder if he does not learn through his own calamities how dangerous any sudden overthrow of divine worship is. <note>original Latin: *Qui univerſam Religionem uno tempore alicujus populi, vel victi, mutare conabitur, mirabor, ſi non per ſuas calamitates non experiatur, quam ſit periculoſum, repentina quæq; Dei cultus everſio.*</note>

But these people have set their minds once and for all and have now brought everything to a head; and what the prophet says in **Jeremiah 48** fits them quite perfectly: **"We have always heard of the pride of Moab, that he is very proud, haughty, defiant, and arrogant."** But the LORD says: **"I know his wrath well, that it is not so powerful, and he presumes to do more than his ability allows."** For once and for all, the Lord **Christ** still lives, and it will be hard for those who persecute Him in His members to **kick against the pricks** <note>Acts 9:5. Likewise, praise God, the honest and pious **Germans** have not yet so completely strayed from their old, inherited, and praiseworthy nature, nor **degenerated** from their innate **frankness** <term>Freymühtigkeit: A traditional German virtue involving both freedom of spirit and the courage to speak the truth to power.</term>, that they would allow themselves to be enslaved in such a **barbaric** manner like those **Persians**, who were such **slavish souls** <note>original Latin: *ſerviles animæ*</note> that when their King (as he sometimes used to do for mere amusement) had one or another of them—whoever was closest at hand—soundly **beaten with a stick** <term>baſtiniren: To administer the bastinado, a form of corporal punishment where the victim is beaten with a staff or stick.</term>, they thanked His Royal Majesty most submissively for it and still considered themselves fortunate and happy that their most gracious sovereign had even been mindful of them.

Meanwhile, dear reader, the Roman-Spanish **intention** and the open **execution** of the **Council of Trent** <note>The Council of Trent (1545–1563) was the formal Roman Catholic response to the Protestant Reformation, which Protestants viewed as a plan for their total suppression.</note> proceed more visibly day by day and are put into action; nevertheless, they are painted over with other colors...

And in the third chapter, the **plausible and specious professions of sincerity** <note>original Latin: *plausibiles & speciosas sincerationes*</note>—or rather, and in effect, the **equivocations** <note>original Latin: *æquivocationes*</note> and deceptive, double-tongued, lying word-play—which they have used here, or intend to use in their minds and whole hearts.

->Which contains several high assurances and promises / which<-

### Extract from the Treaty of Passau <note>The Peace of Passau (1552) was a landmark agreement that granted temporary religious freedom to Lutherans in the Holy Roman Empire, paving the way for the Peace of Augsburg in 1555.</note>,
### established in the year 1552, regarding the matter <note>original: *in puncto*</note> of religion.

As for the subsequent other articles which were raised during this peace negotiation by the **Elector of Saxony** and his associates—specifically concerning religion, peace, and law—**His Imperial Majesty** <note>Refers to Emperor Charles V.</note> shall faithfully follow through on the **gracious offer** which was recently made on His Majesty's behalf at Linz, according to the content of the answer given at that time. He shall also hold a general **Imperial Diet** <note>original: *Reichstag*; the supreme deliberative assembly of the Holy Roman Empire.</note> within half a year. At this assembly, it shall be deliberated by what means—namely through a **General or National Council**, a **Colloquy** <note>A formal meeting for theological discussion.</note>, or a general Imperial assembly—the religious discord might be resolved and brought to a **Christian reconciliation**. Thus, such religious unity shall be promoted by all **Estates** <note>The "Estates" (*Stände*) were the various territories and ranks (princes, counts, cities) represented in the Imperial Diet.</note> of the Holy Empire, together with His Majesty, in an orderly fashion.

Furthermore, to prepare for such reconciliation, a committee of several impartial, sensible persons from both sides and religions shall be appointed in equal numbers at the very beginning of said Imperial Diet. They shall be commanded to deliberate on how such a reconciliation might most suitably be undertaken; however, this committee shall not infringe upon the sovereignty of the **Electors** <note>The highest-ranking princes of the Empire who held the right to elect the Emperor.</note>.

And in the meantime, neither **His Imperial Majesty** nor We, the **Electors**, Princes, and **Estates** of the Holy Empire, shall—on account of religion—press, attack, damage, burden through mandate, or despise any Estate adhering to the **Augsburg Confession** <note>The primary confession of faith of the Lutheran Church, presented to the Emperor in 1530.</note> by force or in any other way against their **conscience** <note>original: *Consciens*</note> and will. Rather, they shall be left to remain peacefully and calmly in their religion and faith, etc.

<term>Passawischer Vertrag</term> — The Treaty of Passau (1552), which ended the Schmalkaldic War and provided a legal basis for Lutheranism.
<term>Reichstag</term> — The Imperial Diet, the assembly of the Holy Roman Empire's power-holders.
<term>Augspurgische Confession</term> — The Augsburg Confession, the foundational document of Lutheran theology.

## Elector of Saxony / dated Vienna / June 6th Old Style / 16th New Style /
## in the year 1620.
### Concerning the granting of religious freedom / in Bohemia.

Although the execution commission sent to you regarding Our Kingdom of Bohemia and several incorporated lands only spoke of privileges in a general sense <note>original: *in genere*</note> to those who would return to proper obedience, and thus no specific mention of the **Letter of Majesty** <note>The *Majestätsbrief* (1609) was a crucial document signed by Emperor Rudolf II that granted religious tolerance to both Protestant and Catholic estates in Bohemia. Its revocation was a primary cause of the Thirty Years' War.</note> or religion was made, all this was done so that no further cause would be given to My and Your Love's enemies <note>"Your Love" (*Eure Liebden*) was a standard formal address used between high-ranking princes of the Holy Roman Empire.</note> to bring their **bloodthirsty, dangerous Calvinist plots** back into play under the guise and cover of the Letter of Majesty, aiming to overturn the entire civil order <note>original: *Policey*; referring here to the general governance and public peace.</note> and the Religious Peace itself. I nevertheless assure Your Love—imperially, sincerely <note>original: *Teutsch*; literally "Germanly," a common period idiom for being honest, plain-speaking, and trustworthy.</note>, and uprightly—that everything I promised, and everything directed toward the Religious Peace in the Empire upon which all else rests, shall be understood as included and shall be effectively fulfilled; furthermore, the **old Hussites** <note>Followers of the reformer Jan Hus; they represented a long-standing Bohemian religious tradition that predated the Lutheran Reformation.</note> in Bohemia shall not be excluded, according to the older agreements.

## From the Imperial Final Decree at
### Regensburg, February 23, 1623.

It has never been His Imperial Majesty's intention, will, or opinion to diminish <note>original Latin: *derogiren*; a legal term meaning to partially repeal or detract from a law.</note> or act against the prominence of the Electors, his own Capitulation <note>The *Wahlkapitulation* was a contract an Emperor signed upon election, promising to uphold the rights of the estates.</note>, the <term>Golden Bull</term> <note>The Golden Bull of 1356 was the primary constitutional document of the Holy Roman Empire, governing how the Emperor was elected.</note>, or other Imperial constitutions in the slightest.

A little later <note>original Latin: *Paulò post*.</note>: Just as His Majesty does not intend to do or permit the least thing that might run contrary to the Golden Bull and his Capitulation, he fully maintains that the <term>Electorate</term> <note>original: *die Chur*; the office and power of one of the seven prince-electors who chose the Emperor.</note> has simply reverted to him, and that he has the right to dispose of it as he pleases.

# C O P Y
## Articles of Peace: Established at Ulm between the Evangelical and Catholic United Estates, on the 3rd of July, New Calendar, in the year 1620.

By the Grace of God, We, Maximilian, Count Palatine of the Rhine, Duke in Upper and Lower Bavaria; and by the same Grace, We, Joachim Ernst, Margrave of Brandenburg, Duke in Prussia, etc. Make it known to everyone that, whereas for a considerable time now, both in the Holy Roman Empire of the German Nation and in various neighboring kingdoms and lands, dangerous and far-reaching conditions and wartime disturbances have appeared; and therefore both the Catholic and Evangelical united Electors, Princes, and Estates have taken occasion to engage in <term>military preparation</term> <note>original: *Kriegspræparation*; the mobilization of troops and resources for war.</note> and defensive arrangements, from which misunderstandings have arisen; along with the concern that the aforementioned military preparations and arrangements made by both Unions were intended for offense, violation, and distress, and thereby to provoke a <term>movement</term> <note>original: *motum*; in this context, an uprising or civil unrest</note> within the Holy Empire. Therefore, so that such misunderstanding might be lifted and better trust established between both Unions and within the Holy Empire, We have—by means of the distinguished ambassadors of His Royal Majesty in France, who happened to be present in the Imperial City of Ulm—agreed upon a certain binding promise, pledge, and assurance.

And firstly, We, Duke Maximilian in Bavaria, as General of the Catholic League <note>The Catholic League was a coalition of Catholic German states formed in 1609 to counter the Protestant Union.</note>, and We, Joachim Ernst, Margrave of Brandenburg, as Lieutenant General of the Protestant Union, do promise, pledge, and assure—also by virtue of the authority we hold, and in the presence of and with the favor and approval of other united Evangelical Princes and Estates, and the authorized counselors, envoys, and ambassadors of those absent, on behalf of ourselves and the Electors, Princes, and Estates devoted to both Unions—by our true words, honor, and faith, in the most powerful and best legal form possible, that neither one nor the other Elector, Prince, or Estate incorporated into either Union shall, in any way or manner, or under whatever sought pretext it might be or

<meta>This page continues the text of the Treaty of Ulm (1620), specifically outlining the non-aggression pact between the Catholic League and the Protestant Union, the rules for troop movement, and the significant exclusion of the Kingdom of Bohemia from this peace agreement.</meta>

can be devised, whether by themselves or through others, using one or another of the <term>military arrangements</term> <note>original: *Kriegsverfassungen*; refers to the legal and structural ways armies were raised and maintained within the Holy Roman Empire</note> belonging to either Union, to offend, insult, or burden the other party and their associated Electorates, Principalities, lands, people, cities, market towns, villages, and possessions in both spiritual and secular matters; nor shall they campaign against, quarter troops upon, assault, disturb, or undertake any acts of violence against one another. Instead, both the Catholics toward the Evangelicals, and likewise the Evangelicals toward the Catholics, shall persevere in rightful, sincere peace, rest, and unity. Each shall allow the other to remain undisturbed and secure in their possessions. And so that this promise and good trust—as is proper in every way between neighboring Princes and Estates according to the <term>Imperial Constitutions</term> <note>the fundamental laws of the Holy Roman Empire</note>—may be steadfastly continued, the soldiery currently held by both sides in the neighborhood shall be removed from the places where they are now stationed as soon as possible, without harm to one or the other, and no other troops shall be lodged in their place.

Secondly, it has been conditioned and agreed that if one or another Elector, Prince, or Estate belonging to either Union—or one of the Unions collectively—should seek <term>passage</term> <note>original: *durchzug*; the legal right for an army to march through a neutral territory</note> for their necessary requirements according to the Imperial statutes, for their own defense and the security of their people, upon providing prior sufficient <term>security</term> <note>original: *caution*; a guarantee or bond to cover potential damages</note>, then neither side shall deny such a request. However, such a request must be made in a timely manner and not unexpectedly or with only a short prior <term>notification</term> <note>original: *avilation*; from the French 'avis,' meaning a formal warning or notice</note> given when the troops are already at the border or already within another’s land, to the distress of the subjects.

And thirdly, whereas We, Duke Maximilian in Bavaria, and other united Catholic Electors, Princes, and Estates, have excluded the Kingdom of Bohemia and its incorporated and other <term>hereditary lands</term> <note>original: *Erbländer*; lands passed down through family inheritance, specifically referring to the Habsburg territories</note> belonging to the House of Austria from this present negotiation; and that into this <term>treaty</term> <note>original: *Tractation*</note> only those Electorates and lands belonging to the united Electors, Princes, and Estates of both sides are included—among which are also the <term>Electoral Palatinate</term> <note>original: *Chur Pfaltz*; the lands of the Count Palatine, a key Protestant leader</note> together with its hereditary lands located within the Empire (because at this time one is not proceeding with others in the aforementioned misunderstanding, but hopefully stands in good understanding with them)—and shall not be extended further: therefore, We, Joachim Ernst, Margrave of Brandenburg, for ourselves and our fellow united Electors, Princes, and Estates, let the matter rest with this declaration of the Catholic Electors, Princes, and Estates, as far as it concerns the Kingdom of Bohemia and its incorporated and other hereditary lands belonging to the House of Austria; and We wish no less...

<summary>This page details the mutual non-aggression pact between the Catholic and Protestant Unions and establishes the rules for "peaceful" troop passage. Crucially, it notes that the ongoing conflict in Bohemia is excluded from this peace, allowing the Catholic League to intervene there without Protestant interference.</summary>

# C O P Y
## Points of Peace: Established between the United Protestant and Catholic Estates at Ulm on July 3, New Style <note>original: "newen C."; refers to the Gregorian Calendar, which was "New Style" compared to the Julian Calendar still used by many Protestants at the time</note>, in the Year 1620.

By the Grace of God, We, Maximilian, <term>Count Palatine</term> <note>a high-ranking noble title within the Holy Roman Empire</note> of the Rhine, Duke of Upper and Lower Bavaria; and by the same Grace, We, Joachim Ernst, Margrave of Brandenburg, Duke in Prussia, etc. 

Make it known to everyone: Whereas for a considerable time now—both in the <term>Holy Roman Empire of the German Nation</term> and in various neighboring kingdoms and lands—dangerous and far-reaching conditions and war-like disturbances have manifested themselves; and consequently, both the Catholic and Protestant united <term>Electors</term> <note>the high-ranking princes who had the right to elect the Holy Roman Emperor</note>, Princes, and Estates have felt prompted to engage in military preparations and organization; and from this, misunderstandings have arisen, as if the aforementioned military preparations and organizations made by both <term>Unions</term> <note>referring to the Protestant Union and the Catholic League, the two opposing politico-military alliances in the early Thirty Years' War</note> were intended for offense, violence, and distress, thereby stirring up unrest in the Holy Empire. 

Therefore, so that such misunderstanding might be resolved and better trust established between both Unions and within the Holy Empire, We have—through the mediation of the honorable Ambassadors of His Royal Majesty of France, who were already present in the <term>Imperial City</term> <note>a city that was self-governing and subordinate only to the Emperor</note> of Ulm—agreed upon a certain binding promise, pledge, and assurance.

And firstly, We, Duke Maximilian of Bavaria, as the General of the Catholic League, and We, Joachim Ernst, Margrave of Brandenburg, as the Lieutenant General of the Protestant Union, by virtue of the power vested in us, and in the presence and with the consent and approval of other Protestant united Princes and Estates, and the authorized Councilors, Envoys, and Ambassadors of those absent, for ourselves and the Electors and Estates belonging to both Unions, do hereby promise, pledge, and assure upon our true words, loyalty, and faith, in the most powerful and best legal form possible, that no Elector or Estate incorporated into either one or the other Union shall, in any way or manner, or under whatever sought-after pretext it may be or

can be devised—neither by themselves nor through others—using one or the other <term>Union’s</term> <note>the Protestant Union or the Catholic League</note> military constitutions, to offend or insult the other party and their associated <term>Electorates</term> <note>territories ruled by a prince with a vote in the imperial election</note>, lands, people, cities, market towns, villages, and holdings in both ecclesiastical and secular matters; nor to burden, overrun, billet troops upon, attack, or disturb them against the religious and secular peace, nor to undertake any hostilities against one or the other. 

Instead, both the Catholics with the Protestants, and conversely the Protestants with the Catholics, shall persist in a righteous, unfeigned peace, rest, and unity. They shall and will let everyone remain untroubled and secure in what is theirs. In order that this promise and good trust—as is proper in every way among neighboring Princes and <term>Estates</term> <note>the constituent territories and rulers of the Holy Roman Empire</note> by virtue of the Imperial Constitutions—may be steadfastly continued, the troops currently held by both sides in the neighborhood shall be led away from the places where they now are as soon as possible, without damage to one or the other; and in their place, no others shall be stationed.

**Secondly**, it has been stipulated and agreed that if an Elector, Prince, or Estate allied with one or the other Union—or either Union collectively—should seek passage according to the Imperial Statutes for their necessary defense and security, upon providing sufficient prior security <note>original: "caution"</note>, the other party shall not refuse such a request. However, such a request must be made in a timely manner and not unexpectedly or with short prior notice <note>original: "avisation"</note> when the troops are already at the border or in another's land, causing a burden to the subjects.

**And thirdly**, since We, Duke Maximilian in Bavaria, and other united Catholic Electors and Estates, have excluded the Kingdom of Bohemia and its incorporated and other <term>hereditary lands</term> <note>territories owned by the Habsburg family by right of inheritance</note> belonging to the House of Austria from this present proceeding; and since this negotiation <note>original: "Tractation"</note> shall comprise only those Electorates and lands belonging to the united Electors and Estates of both sides—including the <term>Electoral Palatinate</term> <note>the ancestral lands of Frederick V; by including them but excluding Bohemia, the treaty effectively isolated Frederick's bid for the Bohemian crown from the protection of the Protestant Union</note> along with its hereditary lands located within the Empire (because at this time one does not deal with others in the aforementioned misunderstanding, but hopefully stands in good understanding with them) and shall not be extended further; 

Therefore, regarding this declaration of the Catholic Electors and Estates, as far as it concerns the Kingdom of Bohemia and its incorporated and other hereditary lands belonging to the House of Austria, We, Joachim Ernst, Margrave of Brandenburg, for ourselves and our fellow united Electors and Estates, let the matter rest, and wish no less...

--- Page 85 ---
<meta>This page continues the specific terms of the Treaty of Ulm (1620), concluding the agreement between the Catholic League and the Protestant Union. It then transitions into a new set of articles negotiated in Mainz in April 1621 regarding the Palatinate conflict.</meta>

Furthermore, the aforementioned Kingdom of Bohemia and its incorporated and other hereditary lands belonging to the House of Austria are not understood to be included under this agreement; instead, such matters shall take their free course. This declaration likewise refers only to the <term>Electorates</term> <note>territories whose rulers elected the Holy Roman Emperor</note>, Principalities, and lands situated within the Empire, as mentioned above.

**Fourthly**, although during these negotiations frequent mention was made of the unresolved grievances <term>Gravamina</term> <note>original: "Grauaminum"; these were formal legal and religious complaints between the Catholic and Protestant states</note> within the Empire, a settlement and resolution has been postponed until a more convenient time. This is due to the lack of time and because such matters concern not only those belonging to the two <term>Unions</term> <note>the Protestant Union and the Catholic League</note> but all Catholic and Protestant <term>Estates</term> of the Empire in general, for whom there was no authorization to act at this time.

Regarding the claims for damages caused by the military forces of both Unions—particularly at Sundheim and its neighborhood—the matter of restitution shall be negotiated in the future according to principles of fairness.

All of this, we, Duke Maximilian in Bavaria, and we, Margrave Joachim Ernst of Brandenburg, have promised to uphold firmly, steadfastly, and inviolably, both for ourselves and for our fellow confederated Electors, Princes, and Estates. For greater security, we have signed this with our own hands and pressed our Princely seals upon it. Given on June 23 (Old Style) and July 3 (New Style) in the year 1620. <note>The "Old" and "New" calendars refer to the ten-day difference between the Julian and Gregorian systems still in use at the time.</note>
