...and their soldiers shall not offend the Lord Marquis Spinola or his army, nor those places under his control, nor others included in this agreement, nor their territories or jurisdictions. Nor shall they assist Count Palatine Frederick with money, troops, or in any other way, **directly or indirectly** <note>original: "directè vel indirectè"</note>, for themselves or through others. They shall neither extend their previously established <term>Union</term> <note>The Protestant Union, a coalition of Protestant German states formed in 1608</note> for the **benefit** <note>original: "fauor"</note> of Count Palatine Frederick, nor against His Imperial Majesty, nor enter into a new one. Instead, following the dissolution of the Union—namely on the 4th or 14th of the upcoming month of May <note>The two dates reflect the difference between the Julian and Gregorian calendars still in use at the time</note>—they shall withdraw and remove both their own and their common troops from the Palatinate and other places used for its protection. They shall then remain loyal and obedient princes to His Imperial Majesty, as they have frequently declared to His Imperial Majesty before. Conversely, the Lord Marquis Spinola shall henceforth **attempt** <note>original: "tentiren"</note> nothing hostile against the persons, troops, officials, subjects, castles, or lands of the Lords of the United Princes and Estates, whether by robbery, force, raids, or in any other way.

In this treaty, both those named in the <term>Agreement of Ulm</term> <note>A 1620 neutrality treaty between the Catholic League and the Protestant Union</note> and other <term>Electors</term> <note>The high-ranking princes who elected the Holy Roman Emperor</note>, Estates, and Imperial Knights of the Empire—whether they be Catholic or Evangelical <note>Protestant</note>—shall be included. However, the aforementioned parties do not wish to make any arrangements regarding the **lands of Jülich** <note>original: "Gülchische Landen"; the succession of these territories was a major flashpoint for conflict in the decades leading up to the war</note> at this time.

However, as far as Count Palatine Frederick is concerned—to whom what has been said so far does not apply—at the **request** <note>original: "requisition"</note> of the King of Great Britain <note>James I, who was Frederick V's father-in-law</note>, the named Marquis Spinola has promised that from now until the aforementioned deadline of May 4th or 14th, he will proceed no further, either by himself or through others, with the assigned **execution** <note>original: "Execution"; the military enforcement of the Imperial ban, which stripped Frederick of his lands and titles</note> against the Palatinate, its people, and its goods. Instead, he will suspend the same, but on this condition: that the United Princes ensure that the aforementioned Count Palatine's own troops, stationed in the Palatinate or in neighboring places, undertake no hostile actions in the meantime against him, Lord Marquis Spinola, his army, the places he currently occupies, or other loyal Estates of the Empire and their servants and subjects.

And so that the neighboring Princes and Estates, and their subjects or others in their territories, are not burdened or harmed by the military forces of either side, the oft-mentioned Princes of both parties have promised to earnestly and certainly ensure that all raids, robberies, or all other damages in their lands and provinces shall be completely forbidden and cease, and that the safety of both the roads and **commerce** <note>original: "Commercien"</note> shall be promoted. ## Recess established in the name of His Imperial Majesty between
## Landgrave Maurice and Marquis Spinola at Bingen
## on April 5, Year 1621.

Be it known that, in the name and on behalf of the Roman Imperial Majesty, also King of Hungary and Bohemia, our most gracious Lord, between the Illustrious Highborn Prince and Lord, Lord Ambrosio Spinola, Marquis of Sesto, Knight of the **Golden Fleece** <note>An elite Catholic order of knighthood; original: "Guͤlden Fluͤsses"</note>, Commander-in-Chief of this Imperial **Army** <note>original: "Exercitus"</note>, on the one part: and then also the Illustrious Highborn Prince and Lord, Lord Maurice, Landgrave of Hesse, Count of Katzenelnbogen, Diez, Ziegenhain, and Nidda, etc., for himself and his Princely Grace’s young heirs on the other part: a treaty and negotiation has taken place regarding the following articles and points for their further assurance. Thus, following the discussions, counsel, and deliberation held, these matters have been negotiated and bindingly concluded by the specially deputized envoys of both sides—whose names are subscribed at the end—with the full knowledge and will of all parties. Namely and first: the aforementioned Prince, the Landgrave of Hesse's Princely Grace, has promised and pledged, for himself or through others, that against His Most High Imperial Majesty and his military forces, he shall provide assistance—neither to the Palatinate of Heidelberg itself, nor to the members of the <term>Union</term> <note>The Protestant Union</note>, nor to other Kings, Electors, and Estates settled within or outside the Empire—who

<meta>This page continues the specific terms of the neutrality agreement (The Recess of Bingen) between Landgrave Maurice of Hesse-Kassel and the Imperial commander Marquis Spinola, following the broader Treaty of Mainz.</meta>

...those who, for the assistance and maintenance of the said Palatinate <note>The Electorate of the Palatinate, then a focal point of the war</note> person and lands, have already entered into the Roman Empire or into the Kingdom of Bohemia along with its incorporated lands, or may hereafter enter: [the Landgrave promises] in no way to assist or side with them **directly or indirectly** <note>original: "directè vel indirectè"</note>, however that might be imagined or named, with people, ammunition, money, provisions, or otherwise with counsel or deed. Instead, both in this and in other occurring cases, he shall steadfastly persevere in the required, most humble devotion and loyalty toward Your Majesty, as is fitting and well-becoming of an obedient and loyal Prince and Estate of the Holy Empire.

Secondly, His Princely Grace has hereby promised and vowed to earnestly recall and discharge all the people together, without delay—if it has not already happened—whom he has held and maintained in the service of the [Protestant] Union <note>The Protestant Union was a coalition of Protestant German states; Maurice was a prominent member</note> until now.

Thirdly, the High-born Landgrave Maurice’s Princely Grace shall, after the expiration of the term ending this coming May, then **immediately** <note>original: "in Continente"</note> completely withdraw from the Union, and henceforth enter into no union or alliance for the said maintenance of the Palatinate and the mentioned lands against the Roman Imperial Majesty and her military forces in the above-indicated Palatine expedition; meanwhile, however, and also thereafter, he shall hold fast and firm to all points contained herein.

On the other hand, and fourthly, the High-born Lord Marquis’s Excellency has promised and vowed that the High-born Lord Landgrave’s Princely Grace and his family, together with their associated principalities, counties, lordships, and inhabited lands, vassals, councilors, servants, and subjects—in their persons, belongings, and property, whether fixed or movable, whether within or outside the country—specifically that the ownership, rents, and income of such property located outside the country shall remain undiminished for them, together with their rights and jurisdictions, trade and commerce, with nothing excluded—shall not be attacked or burdened by his Princely Excellency himself or by his present and future **army** <note>original: "Exercitu"</note> with any violence, invasion, **quartering of troops** <note>original: "Einlägerung"</note>, **extortion of protection money** <note>The practice of "Brandschatzung" involved demanding payment from a town to prevent it from being burned; original: "Brandschatzung"</note>, new tolls, travel fees, taxes, or any other such burdens. Instead, His Princely Grace shall be spared and exempt from all of this, and thus be and remain free and secure. In all other respects, however, both sides shall be left entirely unmolested and at peace according to the established and accepted constitutions and laws of the Holy Empire, and the **Religious and Secular Peace** <note>Referring to the Peace of Augsburg (1555) which provided the legal framework for coexistence between Catholics and Lutherans; original: "Religion vñ Profanfrieden"</note> contained and concluded therein. However, those subjects of His Princely Grace shall not be included herein, nor shall this peace—

In witness and for constant firm observance, the aforementioned deputies and envoys from both sides have signed this with their own hands and knowingly pressed their signet rings upon it. Done and given at Bingen on the 5th of April, after the birth of our dear Lord and Savior Jesus Christ, in the year 1621.

## COPY of a Letter

### From Emperor Ferdinand: To the King in Great Britain / dated Ratisbon <note>Modern-day Regensburg.</note> / the 5th of March 1623. Translated into German from the Latin copy printed in Antwerp / which runs diametrically opposed to the previous resolutions given to England and the Estates of the Empire / concerning the restitution of the Electoral Palatinate.

Since we have been present here at this time for the assembly of Electors and Princes, where we have now remained into the fourth month, we have considered with particular diligence all and every means serving to bring about a universal peace; just as such care is incumbent upon us by virtue of our Supreme Imperial Office, so that the Empire does not remain in constant danger through internal harmful disruption, or through a false and doubtful peace. Therefore, we have also turned our thoughts toward the intercessions made by Your Love <note>original: *Ewer Lieb*; a formal and affectionate style of address used between monarchs of similar rank.</note> with special zeal in various ways, both in writing and through embassies, for the same end and purpose; and we have wished for nothing else than that the whole world might see and know that, whatever could happen without dangerous upheaval of the Empire, We would have gladly done to please Your Royal friendship and the relatives of Your Love.

Now, after extensive and precise consultation with the Electors and Princes, who appeared partly in person and partly through their deputies, we have put forward so much from which it could be well understood that we have no other goal, no other concern, than how common peace and the rest desired by everyone might be restored without infringement of the Imperial constitutions and the beneficial <term>Justice</term> <note>original: *Iustici*, referring to the legal and judicial framework of the Holy Roman Empire.</note> (through whose violation and contempt all kingdoms, even without external force, must necessarily be plunged into ruin). Therefore, we finally opened our mind to the present Electors and Princes and the deputies of those absent as follows: Although the <term>Count Palatine</term> <note>Frederick V, the "Winter King," whose lands were seized by the Emperor.</note> has so far shown no signs of a repentant spirit, we nevertheless—to please Your Love and other Christian Kings and Princes, whose intercessions rightly count for much with us—would not be unwilling to show favor to Your Love's son-in-law <note>Frederick V was married to Elizabeth Stuart, the daughter of King James I.</note> (after he has humbled himself in a fitting manner, acknowledged his guilt, and prayed for forgiveness...)

<summary>This page contains a formal letter from Emperor Ferdinand II to King James I of England, discussing the potential restoration of the Palatinate and the Emperor's desire for a stable peace in the Empire.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the letter from Emperor Ferdinand II to King James I of England. ...and if he <note>Referring to Frederick V, the Elector Palatine, who had been placed under the Imperial ban.</note> continues to refrain from all schemes and military preparations, and offers us sincere loyalty, devotion, and obedience, as well as the required satisfaction, we shall allow him to experience Imperial mercy and grace. Thus, he might find hope for reconciliation and pardon—though the restoration of his Electoral Dignity <term>Electoral Dignity: The right to vote for the Holy Roman Emperor, one of the highest honors in the Empire.</term> is excluded in every way.

Regarding Your Love’s <note>James I’s grandchildren through his daughter Elizabeth Stuart.</note> grandchildren and the relatives of the outlawed man <note>original: "proscribirten agnaten"; refers to the male-line relatives of Frederick V, who was under the Imperial Ban.</note>: just as we never intended to infringe upon or endanger anyone else’s rights through this declaration, we also wish to keep the door of justice open and unbarred for the children (as recipients of our grace) as well as for the brother and the male relatives of the Count Palatine regarding their claimed succession to the Electoral Dignity and its lands.

In the meantime, however, because we have considered the current troubled state of the Empire and all manner of human accidents, it seemed most necessary to us not to leave the vacant position in the Electoral College <term>Electoral College: The council of princes responsible for electing the Holy Roman Emperor.</term> unfulfilled or incomplete any longer. Therefore, with the consent of the present Electors, we have publicly and formally **invested** the person of the Illustrious **Maximilian, Duke of Bavaria** with the Electoral Dignity (which had fallen back to us as a vacant and owned title), both because of his magnificent and unforgettable service to us and the Holy Roman Empire, and for other reasons better explained in our report. 

However, this was done with a hand-sworn promise, expressly included in the deed of investiture, that at a new assembly to be held soon in an Imperial City <term>Imperial City: A city that was self-governing and subordinate only to the Emperor.</term> such as **Nuremberg, Augsburg, Ulm,** or **Frankfurt am Main**, a gracious negotiation shall be undertaken. We wish to promote this to the best of our ability, so that it might be determined what should or could be restored to Your Love’s grandchildren out of grace, and to those relatives claiming rights of succession out of fair judgment, regarding the Electoral Dignity and the lands. Should this effort prove in vain, a summary process <term>summary process: A legal proceeding intended to reach a swift decision by bypassing certain formal delays.</term> shall be held before us and the assembled Electoral College, and everyone can expect prompt and beneficial justice <note>original: "Iustici"</note>, as much as is possible for the parties involved.

Thus Your Love sees (no matter what others may falsely report) what motives moved us to decree these things. They were done to strengthen the foundations of the Holy Roman Empire, to testify to our sincerely inclined Imperial will toward Your Love’s descendants, and to prevent everything that, in these hardships, where the Electoral houses—

...and lest the pillars of the Empire be shaken, or otherwise drawn into a negative consequence, it has been considered most advisable and convenient to proceed without any infringement of existing rights.

We make all this known to Your Love <note>A formal honorific used between monarchs.</note> for this purpose: so that you may perceive that, next to justice and equity, nothing is more important to us than to satisfy Your Love's desires. We trust that Your Love, in accordance with your prudence and integrity, will acknowledge this, our most fair decision, with a thankful spirit—regardless of the slander and machinations of malicious people. Such people, through ill-advised counsel, seek to incite royal and princely persons against each other, hoping to satisfy their own desires through general misery. For we hereby testify that we bear a sincere affection, which shall be most perfectly demonstrated toward Your Love, both in this and all other opportunities (so far as the common good allows). Given at Regensburg on the 5th of March, 1623.

The violations <note>original: "Contraventiones"; refers to breaches of legal agreements or treaties.</note> are quite obvious, yet they are found in the 2nd Chapter, under number 17.

I would have good reason to insert the Bohemian and Silesian **Letter of Majesty** <term>Letter of Majesty: A 1609 document signed by Emperor Rudolf II that granted religious freedom to Protestants in Bohemia and Silesia. Its revocation was a primary cause of the Thirty Years' War.</term> here and to report the violations pertaining to them. However, since they are still fresh in memory and known to the entire honorable world, I have considered it unnecessary and superfluous. Nevertheless, I wish to Christianly admonish the sincere reader to take them to heart.

However, because the Roman Imperial Majesty now claims <note>original: "prætendirt"</note> that the Kingdom of Bohemia is hereditary and that he is therefore authorized to change the religion as he pleases—and because of this, he has moved forward forcefully and compelled the Protestants <term>Evangelicals: In the 17th century, this term generally referred to Lutherans or those following the Augsburg Confession.</term> to attend the Catholic Mass or to vacate their kingdoms and hereditary lands: Therefore, Dr. Hoe <note>Matthias Hoe von Hoenegg (1580–1645), the influential and controversial Lutheran Chief Court Preacher in Dresden.</note>, the Elector of Saxony's Chief Court Preacher in Dresden, has sent a serious letter of grievance to the Prince of Liechtenstein. In this letter, various justifications <note>original: "sincerationes"</note> as well as their violations <note>original: "Contraventiones"</note> can be found. Therefore, I have wished to share this with the Christian reader in its place, as a true "remover of cataracts" <note>original: "Staarenstecher"; a historical term for an eye surgeon who treated cataracts, used here metaphorically for someone who reveals the painful truth.</note>.

So that the favorable reader may grasp and feel with his hands and feet how absolutely nothing can be built upon or trusted regarding their frequently and much-touted declarations of sincerity <note>original: "sincerationes"</note>, I have decided to show him, as if in a mirror, the revocation <note>original: "revocation"</note> document of Emperor Charles V regarding the permitted Lutheran religion.

From this, one may learn how easily they can break, overturn, and nullify all pledges and promises—no matter how highly they were declared "sincere" <note>original: "sincerirt"</note>—without any violation of their conscience. All of this, I believe, will be quite sufficient for us to recognize their declarations of sincerity <note>original: "sincerationes"</note> and, for that reason, to know how to guard ourselves against them.

Your Princely Grace will have noted my most recent letter, and I ask that you again bear with me as I obediently approach Your Princely Grace regarding this highly important matter. I cannot write enough at the outset about how deeply the Protestant <note>original: "Evangelische"</note> powers of the Holy Roman Empire are dismayed and disturbed <note>original: "alteriert"</note> by the ongoing nullification <note>original: "Cassierung"</note> of the Protestant religion in Bohemia, and particularly by the closing of the two newly built Prague churches. I have learned of this with pain from many letters received thus far. Indeed, Your Princely Grace has the high wisdom to judge that the noble Estates of the Augsburg Confession <term>Augsburg Confession: The primary confession of faith for the Lutheran Church, presented to Emperor Charles V in 1530.</term> are not to be blamed for their concern here. For it is not only the Bohemians who are affected; rather, the Imperial Estates themselves have had their churches seized and closed—churches which they built with the permission of Emperor Rudolf II (of blessed and Christian memory), mostly at their own expense, for themselves, for their ambassadors, and for the many thousands of Germans born in their lands and territories who now reside in Prague.

Your Princely Grace is aware of what a loyal, sincere friend His Imperial Majesty has had in my most gracious Elector and Lord, and

<meta>This page continues Dr. Hoe von Hoheneck’s argument to the Prince of Liechtenstein. He is expressing the bitter realization that his trust in the Emperor's protection of Lutherans was misplaced, as they are now being treated worse than groups outside the traditional Christian faith.</meta>

prominent Catholic lords and other distinguished Imperial officers, made orally and in writing—since now a completely contrary result has followed. Thus, Your Princely Grace <note>E.F.G.: Eure Fürstliche Gnaden</note> can judge with high understanding what a great diffidence and mistrust must inevitably arise and follow from this. It goes not only for our religion, which is included in the Imperial Peace of Religion <note>The Peace of Augsburg (1555), which legally recognized Lutheranism in the Holy Roman Empire</note>, no better than for the Calvinists, but even worse than for the Jews themselves. They blaspheme the crucified **JESUS** of Nazareth terribly; they vehemently revile the highly praised and blessed Mother of God and Virgin Mary; yet they are still permitted to have synagogues under His Imperial Majesty’s protection. But we Lutherans, who accept and confess the three main creeds <term>Symbola: The Apostles', Nicene, and Athanasian Creeds, the foundational statements of faith for the early Christian church</term> of the Christian Church, must be driven from all pulpits. Are these not highly precious "sincerities" <note>original: "Sincerationes," referring to formal declarations of honest intent</note> from such highly esteemed "sincerity-givers"? But hear also the zealous accuser; does he not complain loudly against those who did not serve? Had one believed the "Calvinizers," then now the Lutherans would not have to yield their pulpits in Bohemia, Austria, Moravia, and other places. But the "Romanizers" <note>Those favoring the Roman Catholic Church</note> will not do it in vain or without reward.

WE, Charles, etc. acknowledge and make known publicly with this letter: Although some time ago—when we, through the special providence and ordinance of the Almighty and through the unanimous, orderly election of our and the Holy Empire’s Electors, were raised to the honors and dignities of the Roman Empire—until this...

<meta>This page continues the discourse regarding the state of the Holy Roman Empire, specifically reflecting on the Emperor’s attempts to maintain peace and the "German liberties" against the backdrop of the religious schism and the Ottoman threat. It maintains the perspective of Imperial responsibility and the burdens of governance.</meta>

E iij

to this very day / we have let nothing be higher or more important to us than to direct all our senses and thoughts, counsel and deed, toward this: that primarily the glory of God be sought / our holy / true / Christian religion be spread / the common utility / and the prosperity and welfare of the Holy Empire <note>H. Reich: Heiliges Römisches Reich</note> be promoted / increased / and especially that the laudable German Nation be preserved in its rights, justices, old well-established liberties <gloss>original: liberteten</gloss>, freedoms / and all peaceful nature, rest, and unity / and that the occurred / highly harmful / ruinous discord <note>The "zwispalt" refers to the Protestant Reformation and the subsequent religious conflicts</note> in our holy Christian religion be brought back to a Christian reconciliation / and the mistrust that has broken out because of it between the Estates <term>Estates: The "Stände," the various princes, bishops, and imperial cities that held a seat in the Imperial Diet</term> of the Holy Empire be abolished and turned into a friendly reconciliation and goodwill. To this end, we have summoned and held one Imperial Diet <term>Reichstag: The deliberative assembly of the Holy Roman Empire</term> after another / and many times / so that we might attend them / and attend to the affairs and obligations of the Holy Empire / we left our hereditary kingdoms and lands with the greatest inconvenience and instability to ourselves and them / and finally spared neither our Imperial person / nor our resources in this. Inasmuch as we have no doubt / that if all and every one of our actions / carried out in times of war and peace / are considered and placed before one's eyes with diligence and right, true judgment / without envy, hatred, or any particular bias: then as time goes on, this will be found more and more clearly. Yet, alongside this, during the time of our Imperial Reign, the Almighty, out of His divine secret providence, has permitted and let come all kinds of burdensome adversities / not only upon us, but also generally upon all of Christendom (no doubt on account of our sins) / somewhat more and in a more heaped-up fashion than has happened before in any man’s memory / yes, even in several hundred years. Thus, alongside the highly burdensome and disadvantageous discord in our holy religion and faith: the Turk, the hereditary enemy of our Christian name and faith, has greatly increased and expanded his tyrannical power against Christendom from day to day. All Christian kings and potentates have fallen into fierce ill-will / war and unrest / partly among themselves / and partly against us (yet, we hope, without any cause given) / cruel rebellions and uprisings have arisen in all places / and primarily in the Holy Empire of the German Nation / not only from its Estates / but also from the subjects: from which has come the devastation and ruin of land and people everywhere / the shedding of so much Christian, innocent blood / the leading away into captivity / bestial servitude / and in part terrifying

<meta>This page continues the Emperor’s personal reflection on the burdens of his office. Having previously discussed his efforts to maintain the "German liberties" and the unity of the Holy Empire, he now addresses the tragic consequences of the religious schism and justifies the difficult, sometimes compromising, decisions he was forced to make during his reign.</meta>

...apostasy from our Christian faith, from which so much murder, robbery, misery, fear, distress, hunger, sorrow, ruin, and death of poor Christians has followed: and of which there is yet no end or ceasing, which is indeed quite painful and sorrowful to contemplate. However, since the Almighty has nevertheless granted us, out of His mild grace and mercy, all kinds of stately means through which we—insofar as we have had grace and success from Him, and as far as our understanding and ability extend—have exerted ourselves to the highest degree to counter such disorder, and to undertake everything against it that we recognized as our duty by virtue of our incumbent office: 

And as we, amidst the aforementioned adversities, according to the opportunity of the time, had to make a virtue of necessity <note>A common idiom meaning to do what is necessary despite one's preferences</note> and choose the least or smallest of two or many evils, and thus had acted, done, permitted, overlooked, resolved, or executed something on our own behalf that otherwise generally should not have happened: or were at times forced to enter into or approve something that might perhaps bring some injury to our conscience <gloss>original: Gewissen</gloss>, or might be interpreted or construed by others who are not thoroughly informed of the matter, sooner or later, as evil or to our rebuke: 

And then we can testify and swear with the highest truth that we did such things not out of a wicked intention for our own enjoyment or advantage, but entirely out of highly pressing, unavoidable necessity, and to prevent something worse, moved by the aforementioned troublesome times and currents. Also, wherever we might have the opportunity to set the same back on a better and more orderly path, we have applied all human and possible diligence, and would still gladly apply it, as is clearly to be found from many of our conducted dealings and primarily from several of our issued Recesses <term>Recess: From the German "Abschied," this refers to the final official summary of the decisions and laws passed at an Imperial Diet (Reichstag)</term>. 

Thus, for the salvation of our honor and the discharge of our Christian conscience <gloss>original: Conscientz</gloss>, we have seen fit to revoke <note>original: "revociren"</note>, rescind, repeal, abolish, and annul all and everything that we have thus at any time handled, done, approved, overlooked, resolved, and executed for ourselves in matters of religion or faith, or to which we were forced against or beyond our will, which might be in any way against God, our Christian conscience, against Right, our and the Holy Empire’s Recesses, statutes, ordinances, and the Common Land-Peace <term>Land-Peace: The "Landfrieden," a series of legal agreements within the Holy Roman Empire intended to ban private feuds and maintain public order</term>, the Holy Empire’s laudable custom and ancient tradition, as well as honor and equity: And therefore, we publicly protest <note>original: "protestiren"</note> and testify before God and the whole world that all such things happened entirely above and against our good will, out of sheer unjust coercion...

<meta>catchword: or</meta>

<summary>In this passage, the Emperor acknowledges that the religious wars have caused immense suffering and admits to making compromised decisions under pressure. He formally revokes any acts or concessions that violate his conscience or Imperial law, claiming they were made only out of "unavoidable necessity."</summary>

<keywords>conscience, Imperial Recess, Land-Peace, religious conflict, revocation, necessity, Holy Roman Empire, apostasy</keywords>

--- Page 100 ---
<meta>This page concludes the formal legal declaration by Emperor Charles V. It serves as a "protestation"—a legal document intended to clear his conscience and protect his legacy before his impending abdication. He specifically addresses the compromises made during the religious conflicts in Germany, asserting that any concessions made to the Protestants were done under extreme duress and should not be held against him or the Catholic Church.</meta>

or out of the fear of something worse, it has thus transpired and happened: That we, under other circumstances—wherever we might have been able to avoid, prevent, or avert such things with any opportunity and without the highest danger and obvious ruin of the Holy Empire—would not have wished to do it: Where we also might wish to improve anything in the future, we would not want to lack or fail in our highest diligence, effort and labor, expense, and capability: we revoke <note>original: revociren</note>, rescind, annul, abolish, and destroy all such things; we protest <note>original: proteſtiren</note>, testify, declare <note>original: declarieren</note>, and explain ourselves, that such occurred above and against our good will, and was not our mind or intention; and that wherever we could and might have, we would gladly have acted otherwise and better, and henceforth, wherever we only have the opportunity, we would still wish to do so: All this by the fullness of Roman Imperial Power <term>plenitudo potestatis—a legal concept of the Emperor's absolute sovereign authority</term>, with well-considered courage, right knowledge <term>ex certa scientia—indicating the ruler is acting with full awareness of the facts</term>, and our own impulse <term>motu proprio—a legal term for an act taken by a ruler on their own initiative</term>, and by virtue of this letter, in such a way and manner that what was handled by us in an irregular manner, as heard above, shall no longer oblige <note>original: obligiren</note> or bind us, but as far as we are concerned, shall be entirely set aside, destroyed, dead, and gone: 

However, whatever we have otherwise handled, established, ordered, set, or helped to establish, conclude, grant, or enter into with others in an orderly and upright manner for ourselves, shall remain in its force and dignity, constant, firm, and inviolably fulfilled: This is, shall be, and shall remain our earnest, constant, irrevocable, and final will and intention, with which—God willing—we intend to conclude our life: In witness of this letter, sealed with our Imperial hanging seal, and signed with our own hand: Given in our city of Brussels in Brabant: on the 9th day of the month of September after the birth of Christ our dear Lord, 1555.

->Of our Imperial Reign in the 35th and of our Kingdoms<-
->in the 40th year, etc.<-

<meta>This page transitions from the specific legal revocations of Emperor Charles V to a broader critique of Roman Catholic policy. The previous page detailed the Emperor’s claim that his concessions to Protestants were void because they were made under duress. This page introduces "Chapter Two," which argues that the Catholic "Oath of Profession of Faith" creates a fundamental conflict of interest that makes any lasting peace with Protestants impossible.</meta>

[A horizontal ornamental border composed of repeating stylized floral and geometric motifs.]

->No. 1.<-

# The Second Chapter:

## Containing the Roman Catholic violations and offenses against the 
## promises and pledges introduced in the 
## preceding chapter.

### Pope Pius IV’s Bull regarding the form of the 
### oath of the profession of faith.

This oath (which they call the *oath of the profession of faith* <note>original: Iuramentum professionis fidei</note>, that is, the confession of faith) must be taken and sworn not only by the clergy but also by the laity and secular people—of whatever rank, nature, or dignity they may be, and especially by emperors, kings, princes, and rulers—to the bishops or other clergy whom the See of Rome has ordained and delegated for this purpose, prior to all other ceremonies of their coronation or installation. Through this, they are then so closely bound and subjected to the rule and obedience of the Roman Pope that whatever they might do or promise against it—whether by themselves or through others—is held to be null, void, and as though it had never happened or been promised. And this is the sole and foremost fountainhead of all falsehood and perjury by which the Protestants <term>Evangelischen — the term used by followers of the Reformation to describe themselves</term> are accustomed to being deceived; as long as this oath is not abolished, no certain or reliable peace, unity, truce, or alliance—whatever name it may have—can ever again be negotiated or hoped for from the Papists <note>original: Papisten — a polemical term used by reformers to describe those loyal to the Pope</note> for all eternity.

*How this oath and the Peace of Religion <term>Religions fried — specifically the Peace of Augsburg (1555), which legalised Lutheranism in the Holy Roman Empire</term> (which are diametrically opposed to one another) can be kept at the same time, and which of these two obligations a Catholic recognizes as the stricter commitment, let the actions speak for themselves, and let every Protestant judge reasonably for himself.*

<summary>This page argues that the Catholic "Oath of Profession of Faith" required by Pope Pius IV makes any peace treaty with Protestants inherently unreliable, as loyalty to the Pope is positioned above all other legal obligations. It frames this religious oath as the primary source of political "falsehood" and "perjury" in the Holy Roman Empire.</summary>

<keywords>Pope Pius IV, Oath of Profession of Faith, Peace of Augsburg, religious conflict, Protestants, Papists, perjury, political treaties</keywords>

<meta>This page provides the text of the Papal Bull issued by Pope Pius IV, which formalizes the "Profession of Faith" (Professio Fidei) as mandated by the Council of Trent. # Pius IV, Bishop, Servant of the servants of God, <note>original: "Pius der 4. Bischoff/ Knecht der Knecht Gottes." This is the traditional humble title used by Popes, *Servus servorum Dei*.</note> for an everlasting memorial.

It is the duty of our Apostolic service to ensure that everything the Almighty has seen fit to inspire within the Holy Fathers, who were gathered in His name for the prudent governance of His Church, be promptly carried out and put into action for the praise and glory of His name. Since, according to the decree of the Council of Trent, all those who in the future may be appointed to bishoprics and high cathedrals, or provided with their dignities, canonries, and other spiritual benefices to which <term>pastoral care <gloss>original: Seelsorgerey; the responsibility for the spiritual welfare of a congregation</gloss></term> is attached, are required to make a public profession of the true faith and to promise and swear that they will remain in the Roman Church: It is also our will that an identical [oath] be performed by everyone—including those from any foundation, monastery, convent, or other places, and all members of regular, military, or knightly orders who bear any title or name. And so that no lack of care may appear in our presiding office, but rather that a uniform confession of faith may be made by everyone, and a single certain form be maintained and presented to all: We hereby strictly command, will, and order, by virtue of our present Apostolic power and authority, that the formulas contained herein be publicly proclaimed and, throughout the whole world, be accepted, fulfilled, and maintained by those to whom it belongs by virtue of the decree issued in the Council, as well as by others mentioned above. Furthermore, to avoid the penalties determined by the Council itself against transgressors in this matter, only this and no other form shall be used in the aforementioned public confession of faith, as follows:

I, [Name], believe and confess with firm faith everything and each thing contained in the Articles of Faith used by the

...can: I also accept and profess all the traditions, customs, and ceremonies introduced and used by the Catholic Church in the public administration of all the aforementioned sacraments. I agree to all that has been decided, declared, and settled regarding **Original Sin** and **Justification** in the **Holy Council of Trent**. I also confess that in the Mass, a true and proper **propitiatory sacrifice** <note>original: "Sühnopffer"; an offering made to appease divine justice or atone for sin.</note> for the living and the dead is offered to God the Lord; and that in the most holy sacrament of the Eucharist <note>original: "Nachtmahls"; literally "Supper," referring to the Lord's Supper or Holy Communion.</note>, the body and blood, together with the soul and divinity of our Lord Jesus Christ, are truly and substantially present; and that the entire substance of the bread is changed into the body, and the entire substance of the wine into the blood, which change the Catholic Church calls **Transubstantiation** <term>Transubstantiation <gloss>The doctrine that the bread and wine literally become the physical body and blood of Christ during the Mass.</gloss></term>.

I steadfastly believe that there is a **Purgatory**, and that the souls held therein are delivered from there by the prayers of the faithful. Likewise, that the Saints reigning with Christ are to be honored and invoked, and that they offer prayers to God for us, and that their relics <note>original: "Heiligthumber"; holy remains or items associated with saints.</note> are also to be venerated. 

I steadfastly affirm that images of Christ and of the Mother of God, the ever-virgin <note>original: "ungeänderten Jungfrawen"; literally "unchanged virgin," referring to the doctrine of her perpetual virginity.</note>, as well as of other saints, are to be kept and retained, and that due honor and service should be shown to them. I affirm that the power of **Indulgences** <term>Indulgences <gloss>In Catholic theology, the remission of temporal punishment for sins that have already been forgiven.</gloss></term> was left to the Church by Christ, and that their use is highly beneficial to the people of God. 

I recognize the **Holy Catholic Apostolic Roman Church** as the mother and teacher of all churches. I hereby promise and swear true obedience to the **Roman Pope**, the successor of Saint Peter, the Prince of the Apostles, and the Vicar of Jesus Christ our Savior. <meta>This page continues the text of the Roman Catholic "Profession of Faith" (the Tridentine Creed), which was mandated following the Council of Trent. ...used and understood by the Holy Roman Church: namely: I believe in one God, the Father Almighty, Creator of heaven and earth, and of all things visible and invisible. And in one Lord Jesus Christ, the only-begotten Son of God, born of the Father from eternity, God from God, Light from Light, true God from true God, begotten, not made, being of one essence with the Father, by whom all things were created: who for us men and for our salvation came down from heaven, and by the power of the Holy Spirit took on our flesh from the Virgin Mary and became man; he was crucified for us under Pontius Pilate, suffered, and was buried; and on the third day rose again according to the Scriptures; ascended into heaven, sits at the right hand of the Father, and will come again in glory to judge the living and the dead: whose kingdom shall have no end. And in the Holy Spirit, the Lord and Giver of Life, who is worshiped and glorified together with the Father and the Son, who has spoken through the prophets; and in one, holy, universal <note>original: "allgemeine" — referring to the Catholic or 'universal' church</note>, and Apostolic Church. I acknowledge only one baptism for the forgiveness of sins, I hope for the resurrection of the dead, and an eternal life in the world to come, Amen.

I firmly accept and embrace the apostolic and ecclesiastical traditions and ordinances. Likewise, I accept the Holy Scripture in that sense which Holy Mother Church has held and still holds—as it belongs to her to judge the true sense and interpretation of the Holy Scripture—nor will I ever understand or interpret it otherwise than according to the unanimous consent of the Fathers <note>A reference to the "Consensus Patrum," the idea that scripture must be interpreted in harmony with the early church leaders</note>. I also openly confess that there are truly and properly seven sacraments of the New Testament instituted by our Lord Jesus Christ, and necessary for the salvation of the human race (though not all seven are necessary for every individual): namely, Baptism, Confirmation, the Lord’s Supper <note>original: "Nachtmahl" — the Eucharist or Holy Communion</note>, Penance, the Last Anointing <note>Extreme Unction, given to the dying</note>, Holy Orders, and Marriage; and that these confer grace; and that among these, Baptism, Confirmation, and Holy Orders cannot be repeated without sin <note>The Church teaches these three sacraments leave an "indelible mark" on the soul and thus cannot be performed twice on the same person</note>.

...I also condemn, reject, and banish everything that might be contrary to this, and all manner of errors and heresies condemned and banished by the Church.

This present Catholic faith (outside of which no one can be saved, which I hereby freely profess and wish to maintain with truth) I also promise, swear, and vow to keep, with God’s help, firmly and constantly, entirely without doubt and undiminished, until my last breath; not only to hold and profess it myself, but also to ensure that my subjects and those belonging to me, or those under my office <note>The Latin original *curae meae* implies those under the spiritual care or jurisdiction of the person swearing the oath.</note>, as far as in me lies, hold, teach, and preach the same: so help me God, and this His Holy Gospel.

Therefore, let no person at all presume to overturn this our will and command, or to resist it out of intentional boldness. But if anyone should venture to do so, let him know herewith that he will draw upon himself the wrath not only of Almighty God, but also of both His Holy Apostles, Peter and Paul. Given at Rome, at St. Peter's, in the year of the Incarnation 1564, on the 9th of November, in the fifth year of our Papal reign.

->Number 5.<-

### Memorial, presented from the Mainz Chancellery to the Secret Imperial Councilors on September 20, 1613.

His Roman Imperial Majesty <note>Emperor Matthias, who reigned 1612–1619.</note> sees the burdensome and dangerous extremities in which the affairs of the Holy Roman Empire of the German Nation now stand. On one hand, His Imperial Majesty’s scepter is being reached for, and his Imperial authority is being stripped away. His jurisdiction in the Aulic Council and the Imperial Chamber Court <note>These were the two highest courts in the Empire. The Aulic Council (Hoffrath) was directly under the Emperor's control, while the Chamber Court (Cammergericht) was more independent.</note> is not only called into question, but in unfavorable cases <note>original: "in odiosis"; a legal term referring to matters that are restrictive or burdensome</note> is even denied. A new "Princes' Law" is being forced upon the land; justice <note>original: "Iustitia"</note> is blocked; Imperial sentences and matters already decided <note>original: "res judicatæ"</note> are being retracted; the oaths of fidelity and obedience <note>original: "juramenta fidelitatis & obedientiæ"</note> are disregarded. The Imperial Constitutions, the Golden Bull <note>The 1356 decree that functioned as the Empire's constitution.</note>, the Religious and secular Peace <note>original: "prophan Friden"; refers to the Peace of Augsburg (1555) which established legal coexistence between Catholics and Lutherans.</note> are thrown to the wind. Lawful contracts, treaties, letters and seals, reversals, and vows are not upheld due to "reasons of state" <note>original: "per ragion di stato"</note>; and nothing is to be considered right that might hinder the intentions of this side. They claim that in religious matters there can be no judge, that the conscience must not be restricted, and that "Religious Freedom" <note>original: "Freystellung"; specifically the Protestant demand for the right to practice their faith in Catholic territories.</note> must proceed above all else. The clergy are expected to drop their legal actions regarding the occupied <note>The term "occupied" refers to Catholic lands and bishoprics that had been taken over or secularized by Protestant princes.</note> bishoprics and church properties, and a new Religious Peace is to be established. In summary: in both spiritual and worldly matters, no majority <note>original: "Majora"</note> should count, but only the caprice of the "Corresponding" ones <note>The Protestant Union, referred to here as the "Corresponding Estates."</note> and the "sounder part" <note>original: "Saniora"; a legal principle often debated in the Diet regarding whether the "quality" of voters should outweigh the quantity.</note> shall prevail. A new, unheard-of form of government <note>original: "Forma regnandi"</note> from Calvin’s teachings and schools is to be established in the Empire. On the other hand, nothing else is sought 

<meta>catchword: than God-</meta>

<meta>This page continues the "Memorial" or legal memorandum from the Mainz Chancellery to the Emperor's secret councilors. It articulates the Catholic Estates' vision for restoring order in the Empire by strengthening the Emperor's judicial authority and adhering to traditional laws like the Golden Bull.</meta>

...as God’s honor and the *defense* <note>original: "Defension"</note> of his religion, the *Imperial Majesty’s authority and jurisdiction* <note>original: "Keys: Mayst: Authoritet vnd Iurisdiction"</note>, the steadfast *preservation* <note>original: "Conservation"</note> of precious *justice* <note>original: "Iustitien"</note>, unhindered *administration*, and the protection and shield of the burdened. Ultimately, nothing more is desired than that everything which is consistent with the *Imperial Constitutions* <note>The fundamental laws governing the Holy Roman Empire.</note>, the Golden Bull <note>The decree of 1356 that functioned as a constitutional document, detailing the election of the Emperor.</note>, ancient and laudable custom, established *contracts*, and promises be kept inviolably, and that every person be maintained in their own property in peace and quiet.

Since these *extremes* <note>meaning the two potential outcomes of the current political crisis</note> are of such a nature that from one, the *ruin and dissolution of the whole Empire* <note>original: "ruina & dissolutio totius Imperij"</note> and the necessary downfall of the Catholic religion in Germany must follow; and from the other, religion, the Imperial Diet, and the Imperial *authority depend* <note>original: "dependirt"</note>; therefore, the obedient Estates most submissively pray and hope that Your Imperial Majesty, regardless of all contrary impressions and without fearing any imagined *offense* <note>original: "offension"</note>, will most graciously declare yourself in that direction to which you have been directed by God, your conscience, through oaths and duties, by way of office and law, as a properly elected, anointed, and just Emperor.

<margin>Are these not inciting, warlike words? From which one can see which party has for a long time desired to *offend* <note>original: "offendiren"</note> the other.</margin>

Then God, no less than with others, will grant His grace to Your Imperial Majesty’s heroic, Christian, and most praiseworthy *resolutions* <note>original: "resolutionen"</note>. Through this, the restless spirit will be dampened, the Roman Empire secured from the threatened disruption, and the beloved fatherland—to the eternal praise of Your Imperial Majesty—can be maintained in rest and peace. To achieve this goal and *glory* <note>original: "Glori"</note>, there can be no other means than for Your Imperial Majesty to steadfastly *adhere* <note>original: "inhæriren"</note> to the *points of your Imperial Proposition* <note>The "Proposition" was the formal agenda set by the Emperor at the start of an Imperial Diet (Reichstag).</note>, as has laudably happened until now despite all unfair interference, and to help *justice* <note>original: "Iustitien"</note> back onto its feet; for without *justice* <note>original: "Iustitia"</note>, the Empire can no longer be *preserved* <note>original: "conservirt"</note>.

And Your Imperial Majesty should not allow yourself to be misled [by the idea] that the *execution of matters already decided* <note>original: "rerum judicatarum executio"; this refers to the enforcement of court rulings which the Protestant side was increasingly ignoring.</note> would not follow or would be impossible—

<margin>According to the Catholic view, His Imperial Majesty should have pushed through the *execution* against the *Protestants* <note>original: "Evangelischen"</note> in all matters.</margin>

As far as the common defensive system of the obedient Estates <note>The "obedient Estates" are those territories and princes remaining loyal to the Catholic Emperor.</note> is concerned, His Imperial Majesty knows well how to inform himself from the last Recess of Frankfurt <note>original: "Frankfurter Abschied"; a formal decision or decree from an Imperial meeting.</note> that the same is primarily intended for the *preservation* <note>original: "Conservation"</note> of His Imperial Authority, for the administration of precious *justice*, and for the maintenance of peace and quiet in the Empire, and is founded upon mere defense.

<margin>The Catholic League was already forged at the election of Emperor Matthias <note>Matthias was Holy Roman Emperor from 1612–1619.</note>. Because the clergy themselves admit in the preceding memorial that they can reach no agreement <note>original: "Composition"</note> with the Protestants, and yet claim their Catholic League is intended to establish peace and quiet in the Empire, it must follow that they are of the opinion that until the aforementioned heretics in the Empire are rooted out and only one religion is planted therein, no peace can be hoped for in the same.</margin>

And if His Imperial Majesty were able to bring about that they dropped their offensive, forbidden Union or *faction* <note>Referring to the Protestant Union, founded in 1608.</note>: then He is assured that the obedient Estates would gladly withdraw from their forced defense.

<margin>They have now indeed achieved this purpose; nevertheless, the members of the League have not in the least ceased their *offensive* <note>original: "Offensionswesen"</note> activities up to this hour.</margin>

Since, however, it is known to everyone that the Corresponding [Estates] <note>A term for the members of the Protestant Union or those in alliance against the Emperor.</note> will not yield from their misdeeds and unjust *intent*, the obedient Estates expect that His Imperial Majesty will not only not *disapprove* <note>original: "improbiren"</note> of their necessary defensive system, but will *confirm* <note>original: "confirmiren"</note> it with Imperial Authority, and...

# Extract of a Letter of Intercession
## dispatched by a high-ranking Protestant princely person to His Imperial Majesty in the year
## 1616: containing the grievances <term>Gravamina: formal legal complaints or "grievances" presented to the Emperor regarding violations of Imperial law</term> of the German
## Imperial Cities / Regarding the matter of religion <note>original: "In Puncto Religionis"</note>.

Your Imperial Majesty's and the Holy Roman Empire's Free Cities of the Augsburg Confession <note>Protestant cities adhering to the 1530 Lutheran confession of faith.</note> recently brought to my attention through their deputies the manner in which they have been—especially since the time of the prorogued and postponed last Diet <note>The Imperial Diet or Reichstag, which had seen increasing deadlock between religious factions.</note>, despite having always conducted themselves peacefully and quietly—increasingly burdened by their neighboring Papist Estates <note>Catholic territories and princes.</note>, and how they are being denounced as heretics <note>original: "geketzert"</note> through intolerable oppression, contrary to their manifest and glorious rights, freedom of conscience, and tradition.

->Donauwörth <note>A Free Imperial City that was forcibly re-Catholicized and placed under an Imperial ban in 1607 after religious riots. This event was a major catalyst for the formation of the Protestant Union.</note><-

The citizens of Donauwörth are miserably martyred without ceasing, thrown into harsh prisons, burdened with strong garrisons beyond their means, kept from hearing the sermons of God's Word through explicit and severe prohibitions, forced into Roman Catholic baptism and Communion, and are being assailed in countless other ways and manners, which is truly to be lamented and mourned.

->Frankfurt<-

In the same manner, in Frankfurt, into the vacant council position

--- Page 113 ---
<meta>This page continues the specific list of grievances (Gravamina) presented by Protestant Imperial Cities to the Emperor in 1616. It follows the introductory section where the cities complain about the erosion of their rights and the illegal appointment of Catholic officials in Protestant-leaning jurisdictions.</meta>

...once again, the specifically established and irrevocably maintained right and privilege—which was no less publicly proclaimed in the year 1612 and most graciously confirmed by Your Imperial Majesty, and corroborated by the city through a special Oath of Allegiance <term>Huldigungs Aydt: a formal oath of loyalty sworn by subjects to their sovereign</term>—a Catholic person was inserted into office, not without great hardship and danger.

### Aachen.

The free practice <note>original: "exercitium"</note> of the Christian religion has been entirely stripped from the people of Aachen; the majority of the Protestant <note>original: "Evangelischer"; referring to those of the Lutheran or Reformed faiths</note> citizens there have been cast into prison; others, especially school and church ministers, have been driven into misery and out of the country with their wives and children; others who still remain there are oppressed by Spanish soldiery <note>In 1614, Spanish troops under Spinola occupied Aachen to restore Catholic control and suppress the Protestant council.</note>, excluded from the guilds and all honorary offices, and forced into the Papal religion.

### Regensburg.

Likewise, the Imperial privileges of the Council and the entire citizenry of Regensburg—obtained before the establishment of the Peace of Religion <note>The Peace of Augsburg (1555), which established the legal principle of "Cuius regio, eius religio" (Whose realm, his religion), though Imperial Cities had special status.</note> and maintained without disturbance ever since—are being significantly restricted and constrained <note>original: "coarctirt"</note> by the current Bishop of that place.

### Dortmund.

And although in the city of Dortmund, the practice of the Protestant religion was introduced into four churches a good 50 years ago, and the church ministers were provided their salaries from their own funds without involving ecclesiastical estates, and the schools were founded before the Treaty of Passau <note>The Peace of Passau (1552) was a precursor to the Peace of Augsburg that secured Protestant rights.</note>, nevertheless, at the instigation and insistence of a few (though very few) Catholic citizens, a mandate has been imposed upon the Council of that place, ordering them to restore all churches, chapels, and schools, together with their incomes.

### Wimpfen.

In like manner, the Council at Wimpfen has been ordered, upon the appeal of the Cathedral Chapter at Worms, to surrender that church in which the Augsburg Confession <note>The primary confession of faith for the Lutheran Church, presented in 1530.</note> has nonetheless been intro-

<meta>catchword: intro- (German: einge-)</meta>

--- Page 114 ---
<meta>This page continues the list of grievances (Gravamina) submitted by Protestant Imperial Cities. It completes the entry for Regensburg and details specific religious and civil suppressions in Kaufbeuren, Dinkelsbühl, and Weil der Stadt.</meta>

...had been introduced and, in the year 1566, was permitted and confirmed through a special privilege by the most praiseworthy Emperor Maximilian II of most blessed memory <note>original: "Hochseel: Ged:"; an abbreviation for "hochseligen Gedächtnis," used for deceased royalty</note>. It is now demanded that this should be surrendered back to the Catholic citizens, of whom no more than 6 or 8 are to be found there.

### Kaufbeuren.

Likewise, the Council and the citizenry of the Augsburg Confession <note>Lutheran Protestants who follow the 1530 confession of faith.</note> in Kaufbeuren—of whom there are around 500—have been ordered by a mandate to vacate the parish and all other churches for the 50 Catholic citizens residing there. This is required despite the fact that the practice <note>original: "Exercitium"</note> of the Augsburg doctrine had already been introduced there in the year 1544 (which was 11 years before the establishment of the Peace of Religion <note>The Peace of Augsburg, 1555.</note>) and was renewed in the year 1557 after the Interim <note>The Augsburg Interim (1548) was a temporary imperial decree that forced many Protestants to return to Catholic practices.</note> without any opposition from the Roman Catholics.

### Dinkelsbühl.

No less so in the city of Dinkelsbühl, the Protestant citizens (though they lack nothing in quality and skill) are being excluded from the Council and other municipal offices, as well as from charitable alms and the hospital. Foreigners and Catholics are preferred over them in these matters. The schools are being assigned to the Catholics <note>original: "zugethan"; meaning the schools were placed under exclusive Catholic control</note>, and the Protestants have had their official salaries withdrawn. This is despite the fact that, both before and after the established Peace of Religion, matters were handled differently until now; furthermore, the number of Protestants far exceeds the others, and they are far more diligent and industrious in promoting the common good.

### Weil der Stadt.

In Weil, the schoolmasters of the Augsburg Confession are being removed from their posts, and those adhering to this Confession are being expelled from the Council and other offices. To those who marry within the city, all access to municipal service is blocked and denied. Furthermore, it has been ordered by a commission that in both church and school offices, none other than Catholics should be tolerated or admitted, with an attached threat should anyone convert to the Augsburg Confession.

<meta>This page continues the list of religious grievances from various Imperial Cities. The previous page detailed the exclusion of Protestants from civil offices in Dinkelsbühl and began an entry on Weil der Stadt regarding the forced sale of property by those who refuse to convert.</meta>

...be given / the same person would be deprived of their citizenship / and should sell his property within a year’s time.

## Biberach.

In Biberach, the Protestant <note>original: "Evangelische"</note> citizens—who nonetheless constitute the majority—are not admitted to the Mayor’s office or other high positions <note>original: "Digniteten"</note> alongside the Catholics, even though the praiseworthy Emperor FERDINAND provided otherwise in the year 1563 at Constance.

## Hagenau.

The Protestant citizens in Hagenau experience the same kind of exclusion. Catholic overseers have been appointed for the schools, while vacant Protestant positions are not refilled with other Protestants. Financial support for the remaining ones has been withdrawn, while in the meantime, the new calendar <note>The Gregorian calendar, introduced by the Pope in 1582, which many Protestants initially resisted as a sign of Catholic authority.</note> has been introduced, and the Protestants are accused of rebellion and defiance.

## Worms.

In Worms, quite contrary to ancient custom, the Jesuits have recently been brought back. A new school has been built and opened by them under the Bishop’s protection and patronage <note>original: "patrocinio"</note>.

## Nuremberg.

Both the Bishops of Bamberg and Eichstätt are attempting to challenge the status of the churches in the city of Nuremberg that were reformed before the Treaty of Passau <note>A 1552 agreement that served as a precursor to the Peace of Augsburg, securing rights for Lutherans.</note>. They also dispute the right of patronage <term>jus patronatus; the legal right of a patron to nominate a candidate for a vacant ecclesiastical office</term> of those churches, especially along the borders. They seek to appoint Catholic clergy in those places and to install and maintain them with armed force. In the same manner, the Bishop of Constance is attempting to introduce the full practice <term>exercitium; the public performance of religious services and rites</term> of the Catholic religion in the city of Ulm, under the guise of an ordinary parish (which was never granted to him), not without making severe threats.

## In General.

Finally and in general, as soon as it occurs to the Catholics...

<meta>This page continues the list of religious grievances from the previous page, specifically detailing how Protestant subjects are pressured into Catholic practice through legal and financial coercion, followed by a specific report on the suppression of Lutheranism in Prague.</meta>

...that whenever one of their subjects at Protestant <note>original: "Evangelischen"</note> locations or places desires one office or another, they immediately have their Special Inquisitors <term>Special Inquisitorn; officials tasked with enforcing Catholic orthodoxy and identifying heretics</term> compel them to the Papal confession and communion and keep them steadfast in it. They further require them to produce an annual certificate of this, signed by a priest. However, those who turn to the Augsburg Confession <note>The primary statement of faith for the Lutheran Church, presented in 1530.</note> and take up residence in the aforementioned places are denied, withheld, or deprived of their civic rights, birth certificates, certificates of manumission <note>Legal documents proving a person was released from serfdom.</note>, and the like—even the release of their paternal and maternal estates and other inheritances due to them.

Many are also quite stubbornly expected—under threat of losing their fief—to seek and request their investiture <note>original: "Belehnung"</note> by swearing an oath <note>original: "Aydis"</note> to the Saints of God, either in person or through a proxy; otherwise, they are forced to sell such holdings.

## Extract of a Letter from the Elector of Saxony to His Imperial Majesty: Regarding the closure of the Protestant Lutheran churches and the expulsion of the preachers in Prague. Dated Straiditz, October 28 (Old Style) or November 9 (New Style), 1622. <note>The "Old Style" refers to the Julian calendar, while "New Style" refers to the Gregorian calendar introduced by the Catholic Church.</note>

However, the exact opposite has occurred, and my well-intended letter has furthered the execution rather than preventing it. For when my letter was delivered to Your Imperial Majesty’s authorized Governor, the Prince of Liechtenstein, on the 14th of this month according to the old calendar, the closing and sealing of both Protestant Lutheran churches took place that very same day. It was announced to the Protestant preachers—some of whom I had sent there myself—that they must depart from the city of Prague and the entire Kingdom of Bohemia within three days. Furthermore, no communion or marriage <note>original: "Copulation"</note> was permitted for those persons previously established and proclaimed <note>Referring to couples whose wedding bans had already been read.</note>. Thus, the exercise of the true and genuine Augsburg Confession, the established religion <note>original: "exercitium veræ & genuinæ Augustanæ Confessionis, fundatæ Religionis"</note>

[re]moved and no longer occupied / but rather might be protected by the aforementioned treaty / they have humbly petitioned us / and offered themselves to us: Although we had indeed hoped / that the Free and <term>Imperial Cities</term> <note>German: "Reichstätt"; cities that were self-governing and subordinate only to the Emperor</note> occupied with <term>garrisons</term> <note>German: "Guarnisonen"</note> / would have fruitfully enjoyed our recommendations / and not been further burdened: yet we are informed / that no such relief has followed / but instead the citizens are further burdened / defamed, disarmed, and must additionally provide for the maintenance of the soldiers / which costs some cities 15, 16, or more or less thousand <term>guilders</term> <note>German: "Gülten"; a standard gold or silver coin of the era</note> monthly. ### Num. 9

## Extract of a letter from the cities of Strasbourg, Nuremberg, and Ulm to the Elector of Saxony, dated November 20, 1622, of similar content to the preceding one.

In such a manner that since then, they have also begun to demand the free **Protestant Imperial Citizens**—a matter completely unheard of in all memory <note>original Latin: "re in omni memoriâ omninò inauditâ"</note>—to be taken from their cities, away from their deeply distressed wives, children, households, and belongings, to build fortifications <term>at Frankenthal</term> <note>A strategic fortress city in the Palatinate that was a center of conflict between Protestant and Catholic forces</note>. They are forced there under threat of corporal punishment and even by painful means and the threat of the noose, placed at the front everywhere, and thus compelled to perform harder and more dangerous work than the hired soldiers themselves; while, on the other hand, the foreign garrisons remain lying safely behind the city walls and moats.

It can also be proven by examples that the money which certain private individuals from the **Electoral Palatinate** had previously lent to the Imperial Cities—and for which they purchased annual interest <note>original Latin: "reditus"; refers to the practice of buying "rents" or annuities as a form of investment</note>—is now suddenly demanded from the cities all at once. Likewise, although a pure, clear, and unconditional universal amnesty <note>original Latin: "universal amnistia"</note> was decreed in the **Aschaffenburg Recess** <note>The "Abschied" or Recess of Aschaffenburg was a regional treaty intended to stabilize the area</note>, some have nevertheless demanded the **records of the Union** <term>from the city archives</term> <note>Refers to the acts of the Protestant Union, a coalition of German Protestant states</note>. A special commission of inquiry <note>original Latin: "Commission ad inquirendum"</note> has been established to investigate those who previously advised joining the Union, and other such past occurrences. Thus, the heart of an honest Protestant man, who personally hears the poor people's lamentations, might well bleed for it.

->Num. 11.<-

# Extract from the Report of the Imperial Vice-Chancellor,
## February 15, 1623, at Regensburg, concerning the transfer of the Electoral Palatinate to Bavaria.

Since it is then known and manifest that the former Elector and proscribed **Count Palatine** <note>Frederick V, the "Winter King," who was placed under the Imperial Ban for accepting the Bohemian crown</note> has not only set himself against His Imperial Majesty as the supreme Head, but has persecuted, insulted, and distressed him to the utmost; and was also the true ringleader of the rebellion that arose—the likes of which has never been heard of in the entire **Holy Roman Empire**—and has thereby fallen under His Majesty’s and the Empire's **Imperial Ban** <note>original: "Acht vnd Aberacht"; a form of outlawry that stripped a person of all legal rights and lands</note>:

Therefore, His Imperial Majesty wishes to enfeoff <note>To formally grant land or titles in exchange for a pledge of service</note> His Serene Highness in **Bavaria**—who is not only descended from the blood of the **Electoral Palatinate**, but has also served His Majesty and the Roman Empire well, and helped His Imperial Majesty recover his lands and people—with the fallen **Electoral Palatinate**, together with its high authorities, regalia, dignities, and honors. This includes the **office of the Arch-Steward** <note>original: "Erztruchsessen Ampt"; one of the high ceremonial offices of the Empire</note>, rights, voice, session, and vote in the manner mentioned above. There is no doubt that His Serene Highness will remain as faithful to His Imperial Majesty as before and show himself to be an obedient, loyal Elector of the Empire toward His Imperial Majesty. 

Whereupon His Serene Highness in **Bavaria** took the customary **oath** <note>original Latin: "Iurament"</note>, thanked His Imperial Majesty for the Electorate, the lands, and all their **dependencies** <note>original Latin: "pertinentien"; referring to all attached territories and rights</note>, and obediently offered to earn such Imperial Grace in return with his body, goods, honor, and blood, and honored His Imperial Majesty with a golden tablet.

<summary>This page records the formal 1623 transfer of the Electoral title and the office of Arch-Steward from the disgraced Frederick V of the Palatinate to Duke Maximilian I of Bavaria as a reward for his military support of the Emperor.</summary>
<keywords>Electoral Palatinate, Bavaria, Imperial Ban, Frederick V, Maximilian I, Arch-Steward, Regensburg, Thirty Years' War, Electoral College</keywords>

->No. 12.<-

### Extract of a Letter from the Electorate of Saxony to the Electorate of Mainz, sent on February 23, 1623, regarding the unusual transfer of the Electoral Palatinate to Bavaria.

What the office of the Electors in the Roman Empire is, and upon what it and their preeminence and highness consist and rest, requires no explanation for Your Charity <note>original: "E. L." or "Euer Liebden," a traditional honorific used between high-ranking German nobles, similar to "Your Grace" but implying a fraternal bond</note> as an old, well-experienced Elector knowledgeable in Imperial affairs. The **Golden Bull** <note>The constitutional decree of 1356 that settled the method of electing the Emperor</note>, the confirmed and sworn union of the Electors, and the observance of tradition handed down over many years demonstrate this in a grand and distinguished manner. The Electors have maintained these rights until now; however, it seems to Us that they are currently suffering no small offense. 

For, to say nothing of the condemnation of the Electors and Princes which occurred at this convention against our reminders—of which no such example exists when the **Electoral College** <note>The elite group of princes who elected the Holy Roman Emperor</note> was incomplete, as it was at this assembly—and which cannot easily be resolved by the protest already made and recorded in the protocol <note>original: "ad prothocollum"</note>, even though it is deemed to require further explanation: 

The Electors should be the innermost and most secret advisors to His Roman Imperial Majesty. They should be consulted in all highly important matters, especially those concerning the **Holy Roman Empire**, and actions should be taken with their prior knowledge. However, Your Charity is aware of how the matter of the **Imperial Ban** <note>original: "Aacht"; a legal status where a person was declared an outlaw and stripped of lands and rights</note> against the Count Palatine (whose deeds and acts <note>original: "facta vnd acta"</note> We still do not approve, as before) and the transfer <note>original: "translation"</note> of the Electoral title proceeded, and it has been sufficiently indicated at this convention. 

If the Electors were not to be consulted in such matters—specifically, when an Elector is placed under the Ban, removed from the Electoral College, and another is put in his place—We would not know what the Electoral highness would consist of, and what difference there would be between them and other Estates <note>The other princes and cities of the Empire who did not have the right to elect the Emperor</note>, besides the name alone? And considering the danger that arises from such lofty matters quite...

<meta>catchword: easily</meta>

<summary>In this letter, the Elector of Saxony protests to the Elector of Mainz about the procedural irregularities in stripping the Count Palatine of his title. Saxony argues that the Emperor’s unilateral action undermines the traditional power and advisory role of the Electoral College as defined by the Golden Bull.</summary>
